\displaystyle g^{\alpha\beta}\,(\partial_{x^{\alpha}}\Gamma_{\mu\beta\nu}-\partial_{x^{\nu}}\Gamma_{\mu\beta\alpha})+g^{\alpha\beta}\,\Gamma_{\nu\lambda\beta}\Gamma_{\mu\alpha}^{\lambda}-g^{\alpha\beta}\,\Gamma_{\beta\lambda\alpha}\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,\Box^{\prime}_{g}\,g_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle+\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\bigg(\frac{1}{4}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\alpha\beta}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{4}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\Big((\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\beta\nu}-\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}\,\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\beta\nu})+(\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\beta\mu}-\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}\,\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\beta\mu})\Big)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{2}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\Big((\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\beta\nu}-\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\beta\nu})+(\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\beta\mu}-\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\beta\mu})\Big)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{2}\,(\partial_{x^{\mu}}\Gamma_{\nu}+\partial_{x^{\nu}}\Gamma_{\mu})+\frac{1}{2}\,\Gamma^{\beta}(\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\beta\nu}+\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\beta\mu})+\frac{1}{4}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}(\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\Gamma_{\nu}+\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\Gamma_{\mu})

\displaystyle+\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,\bigg(\frac{1}{4}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\alpha\beta}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\beta\mu}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha^{\prime}}}g_{\beta^{\prime}\nu}\bigg)

\displaystyle-\,\frac{1}{2}\,\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\,g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+\Gamma_{\mu}\bigg)\,\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}+\Gamma_{\nu}\bigg)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,\bigg(\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\beta\mu}\,\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\nu}-\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\beta\mu}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\nu}\bigg)

\displaystyle-\,\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}\,\Gamma_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,\Box^{\prime}_{g}\,g_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{4}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{4}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\alpha\beta}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{8}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{2}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\beta\mu}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha^{\prime}}}g_{\beta^{\prime}\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,\bigg(\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\beta\mu}\,\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\nu}-\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\beta\mu}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\nu}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{2}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\Big((\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\beta\nu}-\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\beta\nu})+(\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\beta\mu}-\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\beta\mu})\Big)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{4}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\Big((\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\beta\nu}-\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}\,\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\beta\nu})+(\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\beta\mu}-\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}\,\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\beta\mu})\Big)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{2}\,(\partial_{x^{\mu}}\Gamma_{\nu}+\partial_{x^{\nu}}\Gamma_{\mu})+\frac{1}{2}\,\Gamma^{\beta}(\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\beta\nu}+\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\beta\mu})+\frac{1}{4}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}(\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\Gamma_{\nu}+\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\Gamma_{\mu})

\displaystyle-\,\frac{1}{4}\,g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,\Gamma_{\nu}-\frac{1}{4}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\Gamma_{\mu}-\frac{1}{2}\,\Gamma_{\mu}\Gamma_{\nu}-\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}\,\Gamma_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,\Box^{\prime}_{g}\,g_{\mu\nu}+g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\bigg(-\frac{1}{4}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\alpha\beta}+\frac{1}{8}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{2}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\beta\mu}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha^{\prime}}}g_{\beta^{\prime}\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,\bigg(\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\beta\mu}\,\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\nu}-\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\beta\mu}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\nu}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{2}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\Big((\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\beta\nu}-\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\beta\nu})+(\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\beta\mu}-\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\beta\mu})\Big)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{4}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\Big((\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\beta\nu}-\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}\,\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\beta\nu})+(\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\beta\mu}-\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}\,\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}g_{\beta\mu})\Big)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{2}\,(\partial_{x^{\mu}}\Gamma_{\nu}+\partial_{x^{\nu}}\Gamma_{\mu})+\frac{1}{2}\,\Gamma^{\beta}(\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\beta\nu}+\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\beta\mu})-\frac{1}{2}\,\Gamma_{\mu}\Gamma_{\nu}-\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}\,\Gamma_{\lambda},

where we did some index renaming in the last step.
Therefore we have shown the following result.

Lemma 2.2

Let
\Box^{\prime}_{g}=g^{\alpha\beta}\,\partial^{2}_{x^{\alpha}x^{\beta}}
.
The Ricci tensor may be expressed as

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,\Box^{\prime}_{g}\,g_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{2}\,\tilde{P}(g;\partial_{x^{\mu}}g,\partial_{x^{\nu}}g)+\frac{1}{2}\,\tilde{Q}_{\mu\nu}(g;\partial g,\partial g)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{2}\,(\partial_{x^{\mu}}\Gamma_{\nu}+\partial_{x^{\nu}}\Gamma_{\mu})+\frac{1}{2}\,\Gamma^{\beta}(\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\beta\nu}+\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\beta\mu})-\frac{1}{2}\,\Gamma_{\mu}\Gamma_{\nu}-\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}\,\Gamma_{\lambda},

where

\displaystyle\tilde{P}(g;\partial_{x^{\mu}}h,\partial_{x^{\nu}}k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{x^{\mu}}h_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\partial_{x^{\nu}}k_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}h_{\alpha\beta}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}k_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}},

(B.31)

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}_{\mu\nu}(g;\partial h,\partial k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha}}h_{\beta\mu}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha^{\prime}}}k_{\beta^{\prime}\nu}

(B.32)

\displaystyle-\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,(\partial_{x^{\alpha}}h_{\beta\mu}\,\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}k_{\alpha^{\prime}\nu}-\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}h_{\beta\mu}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha}}k_{\alpha^{\prime}\nu})

\displaystyle+\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,(\partial_{x^{\mu}}h_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha}}k_{\beta\nu}-\partial_{x^{\alpha}}h_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}k_{\beta\nu})

\displaystyle+\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,(\partial_{x^{\nu}}h_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha}}k_{\beta\mu}-\partial_{x^{\alpha}}h_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}k_{\beta\mu})

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{2}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,(\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}h_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\mu}}k_{\beta\nu}-\partial_{x^{\mu}}h_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}h_{\beta\nu})

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{2}\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,(\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}h_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\nu}}k_{\beta\mu}-\partial_{x^{\nu}}h_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,\partial_{x^{\beta^{\prime}}}k_{\beta\mu}).

In particular, in the harmonic gauge one has

R_{\mu\nu}=-\frac{1}{2}\,\Box^{\prime}_{g}\,g_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{2}\,\tilde{P}(g;\partial_{x^{\mu}}g,\partial_{x^{\nu}}g)+\frac{1}{2}\,\tilde{Q}_{\mu\nu}(g;\partial g,\partial g).

(B.33)

Since
g^{\mu\nu}g_{\mu\nu}=4
and hence
R=-\frac{8\pi}{c^{4}}\,{\rm tr}_{g}(T)
,
we also have the following consequence.

Corollary 2.3

Let
\Box^{\prime}_{g}=g^{\alpha\beta}\,\partial^{2}_{x^{\alpha}x^{\beta}}
.
In the harmonic gauge, the Einstein equations become

\Box^{\prime}_{g}\,g_{\alpha\beta}=\tilde{P}(g;\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g,\partial_{x^{\beta}}g)+\tilde{Q}_{\alpha\beta}(g;\partial g,\partial g)-\frac{16\pi G}{c^{4}}\bigg(T_{\alpha\beta}-\frac{1}{2}\,{\rm tr}_{g}(T)\,g_{\alpha\beta}\bigg).

Appendix C
Appendix: Discussion on Blanchet’s condition

When solving the wave equation (
2.49
) that results from the choice
of the harmonic gauge, a term of the form

S_{\alpha\beta}(t,x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{|x-\bar{x}|}\,\phi_{\alpha\beta}(t-c^{-1}|x-\bar{x}|,\bar{x})\,d\bar{x}

(C.1)

occurs in (
2.51
), where
\phi_{\alpha\beta}
is quadratic in
h
and/or its derivatives.
The quantity
S_{\alpha\beta}
describes the gravitational part of the source term
of the wave equation, in contrast to what happens in electrodynamics, where this source term
is due to matter only and has compact spatial support. In most physics textbooks, (
C.1
)
is swept under the rug as a term of “higher order in the weak-field approximation
and therefore dispensable”.

However, to those working in gravitational radiation (cf.
[
17
, p. 54]
for instance),
it is well-known that (
C.1
) will generally be infinite without further assumptions.
Let us briefly explain why this could happen, from a mathematics viewpoint. For simplicity
we drop
\lambda
,
c
and other constants in the short argument to follow. Let
(t,x)

be fixed such that
t\geq 0
,
|t-|x||\leq 1
and
|x|\geq 1
, and define the set

A=\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:|z|\geq|x|\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\langle z,x\rangle\leq 0\},

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denotes the Euclidean inner product.
The relation
|x-z|^{2}\geq|x|^{2}+|z|^{2}
holds for
z\in A
, and together with
|x|\geq 1

it leads to
|x-z|^{2}\geq(|z|-|x|+1)^{2}
and thus
|x-z|\geq||z|-|x|+1|=|z|-|x|+1\geq|z|-t
.
Furthermore, we also have
|x-z|\geq|z|-|x|+1\geq|x|-|z|+1\geq t-|z|
.
In conclusion,
|x-z|\geq|t-|z||
is verified for all
z\in A

and from what we know about the decay of
h
(or its derivatives),
one can therefore expect an estimate like

\displaystyle|S_{\alpha\beta}(t,x)|

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{|x-\bar{x}|}\,|h(t-|x-\bar{x}|,\bar{x})|^{2}\,d\bar{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{|z|}\,|h(t-|z|,x-z)|^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\int_{A}\frac{1}{|z|}\,\dfrac{(1+|t-|z||)^{2\delta}}{(1+|x-z|+|t-|z||)^{2}(1+|x-z|-|t-|z||)^{2\gamma}}\,dz

to hold. Now
1+|x-z|-|t-|z||\leq 1+|x|+|z|+t-|z|=1+|x|+t
yields

|S_{\alpha\beta}(t,x)|\gtrsim\frac{1}{(1+|x|+t)^{2\gamma}}\int_{A}\frac{1}{|z|}\,\dfrac{(1+|t-|z||)^{2\delta}}{(1+|x-z|+|t-|z||)^{2}}\,dz.

However, for points
z\in A
with
|z|
large, this integrand behaves
like
|z|^{2\delta-3}
, causing the integral to diverge even if
\delta=0
, due to the fact that
A
is a half space
minus a bounded half ball.

Blanchet
[
3
, equ. (19)]
defines a
no-incoming-radiation condition

on the metric of a spacetime. First note that the expression
that he calls the
(inverse) metric perturbation
,

h_{\text{Blanchet}}^{\alpha\beta}:=\sqrt{|g|}g^{\alpha\beta}-\eta^{\alpha\beta}\ ,

actually differs from our inverse metric perturbation (which is the same as for
[
9
]
):

h^{\alpha\beta}=\eta^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\eta^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}h_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}=\eta^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\eta^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}(g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}-\eta_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}});

see Section
1.3.1
. Blanchet’s condition is written as follows:
Some large enough
\mathcal{T}>0
exists such that,
for all
\alpha,\beta=0,1,2,3
,

\partial_{t}(h_{\text{Blanchet}}^{\alpha\beta})=\partial_{t}\left(\sqrt{|g|}g^{\alpha\beta}-\eta^{\alpha\beta}\right)=0\quad\text{at all points }(t,x)\text{ with }t\leq-\mathcal{T}\ .

(C.2)

We can rephrase this as: For each
\alpha,\beta=0,1,2,3
there exists a function

q^{\alpha\beta}=q^{\alpha\beta}(x)
such that

q^{\alpha\beta}(x)+\eta^{\alpha\beta}=(\sqrt{|g|}g^{\alpha\beta})(t,x)\quad\text{for all }t\leq-\mathcal{T}\ .

(C.3)

Physically meaningful asymptotically flat spacetimes will have
q^{\alpha\beta}
bounded over the whole domain
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
More than that,
q^{\alpha\beta}
should decay as
|x|\to\infty
,
along with its derivatives, with decay rates given as

|\partial^{\kappa}q^{\alpha\beta}(x)|\sim|x|^{-(1+|\kappa|)}

for
|x|\geq{\cal S}
and
|\kappa|\leq 3
in the case of an isolated body.

In order to compare this to the NIR condition (
1.23
), all that is needed
is the following lemma that relates the
q
-terms above to the
p
-terms
that appear in NIR.

Lemma 3.1

Under the hypothesis of (
C.3
), we have

h_{\alpha\beta}(t,x)=-q_{\alpha\beta}(x)+\frac{1}{2}\,q(x)\,\eta_{\alpha\beta}+{\cal O}(q^{2},hq)\ ,

(C.4)

where we defined

q_{\alpha\beta}=\eta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\eta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}q^{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\ ,\quad q=q_{\alpha\beta}q^{\alpha\beta},

and where
{\cal O}(q^{2})
represents remainder terms that are at least quadratic
in
q^{\alpha\beta}
terms or proportional to
h_{\alpha\beta}q^{\mu\nu}
terms.

Proof :
Consider the matrices of functions

\hat{q}=(q^{\alpha\beta})\ ,\quad\hat{m}^{-1}=\hat{m}=(\eta^{\alpha\beta})=(\eta_{\alpha\beta})\ ,\quad\hat{g}=(g_{\alpha\beta})\ ,\quad\hat{H}=(H^{\alpha\beta})\ ,

where
H^{\alpha\beta}=g^{\alpha\beta}-\eta^{\alpha\beta}

is as in (
1.17
). Then (
C.3
) is written as

\hat{q}+\hat{m}^{-1}=\sqrt{|\det\hat{g}|}\ \hat{g}^{-1}\ .

In particular

\displaystyle\det(\hat{q}+\hat{m}^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sqrt{|\det\hat{g}|}\right)^{4}(\det\hat{g})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sqrt{|\det\hat{g}|}\right)^{4}\left(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{|\det\hat{g}|}\sqrt{|\det\hat{g}|}}\right)=-\left(\sqrt{|\det\hat{g}|}\right)^{2},

so that
\sqrt{|\det\hat{g}|}=\sqrt{-\det(\hat{q}+\hat{m}^{-1})}
and hence

\hat{g}^{-1}=\frac{\hat{q}+\hat{m}^{-1}}{\sqrt{-\det(\hat{q}+\hat{m}^{-1})}},\quad\hat{H}=-\hat{m}^{-1}+\frac{\hat{q}+\hat{m}^{-1}}{\sqrt{-\det(\hat{q}+\hat{m}^{-1})}},

or, what is the same,

H^{\alpha\beta}=-\eta^{\alpha\beta}+(-\det(\eta^{\mu\nu}+q^{\mu\nu}))^{-1/2}(\eta^{\alpha\beta}+q^{\alpha\beta})\ .

The determinant appearing on the right side, to first order in
q
terms, is

-\det(\eta^{\mu\nu}+q^{\mu\nu})=-\det(\hat{m}^{-1}+\hat{q})=-\det(\hat{m}^{-1})\det(1+\hat{m}\hat{q})\approx 1+\mathrm{tr}(\hat{m}\hat{q})=1+\eta_{\mu\nu}q^{\mu\nu}\ .

With this, the formula for
H^{\alpha\beta}
to first order in small quantities becomes

H^{\alpha\beta}\approx-\eta^{\alpha\beta}+\left(1-\frac{\eta_{\mu\nu}q^{\mu\nu}}{2}\right)(\eta^{\alpha\beta}+q^{\alpha\beta})\approx q^{\alpha\beta}-\frac{1}{2}q\eta^{\alpha\beta}\ .

(C.5)

Now we can find
h_{\alpha\beta}
in terms of
H^{\mu\nu}
. The relation

\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}=g^{\alpha\mu}g_{\mu\beta}=(\eta^{\alpha\mu}+H^{\alpha\mu})(\eta_{\mu\beta}+h_{\mu\beta})=\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}+g^{\alpha\mu}h_{\mu\beta}+H^{\alpha\mu}\eta_{\mu\beta}

yields
g^{\alpha\mu}h_{\mu\beta}=-H^{\alpha\mu}\eta_{\mu\beta}
and therefore

-g_{\nu\alpha}H^{\alpha\mu}\eta_{\mu\beta}=g_{\nu\alpha}g^{\alpha\mu}h_{\mu\beta}=\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}h_{\mu\beta}=h_{\nu\beta},\quad h_{\alpha\beta}=-g_{\alpha\mu}\eta_{\beta\nu}H^{\mu\nu}\ .

To first order, we can replace the
g
term by an
\eta
term. Then,
by plugging in (
C.5
), we find

h_{\alpha\beta}\approx-\eta_{\alpha\mu}\eta_{\beta\nu}\left(q^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}q\eta^{\mu\nu}\right)=-q_{\alpha\beta}+\frac{1}{2}q\eta_{\alpha\beta}\ ,

which yields the claim.

\Box

Thus, to summarize, the assumption (
1.23
) that we use as a
no-incoming-radiation condition

agrees to first order in
h
(which is the relevant order for the quadrupole formula)
with Blanchet’s original condition (
C.2
), with some natural decay assumptions added
on the functions
p_{\alpha\beta}(x)
for large
|x|
. We also remark that a factor of
\varepsilon_{h}
has been added
to the right-hand side of (
1.23
). This accounts for the fact that the smallness
of the perturbation
h
is measured in terms of
\varepsilon_{h}
.

Appendix D
Appendix: Bounds derived from
[
9
]

This section describes how to adapt the relevant estimates from
[
9
]
, where nothing
has a physical dimension, to our desired context where factors of
c
and
G
are kept.
These estimates are only stated for reference, as a justification for our assumptions
in Subsection
1.3
, but they are not further used in this paper
(apart from the method of
[
9
, Prop. 2.1]
to control the support of
f(t)
,
which will appear in Subsection
E.1.6
below).

D.1
Description of relevant results from
[
9
]

Here we summarize and paraphrase the results from
[
9
]
that we need. Let
({\cal M},g)

be a Lorentzian
4
-manifold with signature
(-,+,+,+)
and a coordinate system

(x^{0},x^{1},x^{2},x^{3})
in the harmonic gauge. Let
(x^{\mu},p^{\nu})=(x^{0},x^{1},x^{2},x^{3},p^{0},p^{1},p^{2},p^{3})
denote the natural coordinates on
T{\cal M}
. Given
(x^{\mu})\in M
,
we let
r^{2}=(x^{1})^{2}+(x^{2})^{2}+(x^{3})^{2}
. For a tensor
U_{\alpha\beta}
,
the notation
|U(x^{\mu})|
means
\sum_{\alpha,\beta}|U_{\alpha\beta}(x^{\mu})|
,
while
\lVert U(x^{0},\cdot)\rVert_{L^{2}}

means
\sum_{\alpha,\beta}\lVert U_{\alpha\beta}(x^{0},\cdot)\rVert_{L^{2}}

(and similarly for tensors of any rank). Define the mass shell

P{\cal M}=\{(x^{\mu},p^{\nu})\in T{\cal M}\ :\ (p^{\nu})\text{ is future-directed, }g_{\alpha\beta}p^{\alpha}p^{\beta}=-1\}\ .

For a fixed
x=(x^{\mu})\in{\cal M}
define also

P{\cal M}(x)=\{(p^{\nu})\in T_{x}{\cal M}\ :\ (p^{\nu})\text{ is future-directed, }g_{\alpha\beta}p^{\alpha}p^{\beta}=-1\}\ .

Note that the dependence of
P{\cal M}(x)
on
x
enters through the
g_{\alpha\beta}
terms.
Let
f:P{\cal M}\longrightarrow[0,\infty)
be a mass-density scalar function
and define the energy-momentum tensor

T^{\mu\nu}(x)=|g(x)|^{1/2}\int_{P{\cal M}(x)}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\,f(x,p^{\beta})\,\frac{1}{-p_{0}}\,dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}\ ,

(D.1)

where
g<0
denotes the metric determinant,
x
denotes a spacetime 4-vector
(x^{\alpha})
,
the symbols
p^{1},p^{2},p^{3}
are to be regarded as mere integration variables,
and
p^{0}
and its lowered counterpart
p_{0}=g_{\alpha 0}\,p^{\alpha}

are functions of
p^{1},p^{2},p^{3}
implicitly defined by the relation

g_{\alpha\beta}\,p^{\alpha}p^{\beta}=-1
. [We remark that the formula for
T^{\mu\nu}

actually presented in
[
9
]
differs from (
D.1
) above in that it doesn’t have
the minus sign and it shows
p^{0}
instead of
p_{0}
in the denominator,
but this details doesn’t change any of the estimates that we need.
See Section
1.1
with
c=G=m=1
and
[
1
]
for a consistent set of equations.]
Finally, letting
X
denote the generator of geodesic flow on
{\cal M}
,
which is the vector field on
T{\cal M}
given by

X(x^{\mu},p^{\nu})=p^{\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}-p^{\alpha}p^{\beta}\Gamma^{\mu}_{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial}{\partial p^{\mu}}\ ,

where
(x^{\mu},p^{\nu})
refer to the canonical coordinates of the tangent bundle as above, then the Vlasov equation for
f
reads:

X(f)=0\ .

(D.2)

The so-called
reduced Einstein-Vlasov system
is composed of equation (
D.1
) and (
D.2
)
together with the Einstein equations, which
[
9
]
write in the equivalent trace-reduced formulation:

\mathrm{Ric}(g)_{\alpha\beta}=T_{\alpha\beta}-\frac{1}{2}\,g_{\alpha\beta}\,\mathrm{tr}_{g}T\ .

(D.3)

In
[
9
]
this equation is expanded in the following form:

\Box^{\prime}_{g}\,g_{\alpha\beta}=F_{\alpha\beta}(g)(\partial g,\partial g)+T_{\alpha\beta}-\frac{1}{2}\,{\rm tr}_{g}(T)\,g_{\alpha\beta}

for
\Box^{\prime}_{g}=g^{\alpha\beta}\,\partial_{x^{\alpha}}\partial_{x^{\beta}}
.
Here a factor of
-16\pi
is missing on the
T
-terms (see Corollary
2.3
),
and the function
F_{\alpha\beta}
turns out to be the following quadratic expression:

F_{\alpha\beta}(g)(\partial g,\partial g)=\tilde{P}(g;\partial_{x^{\alpha}}g,\partial_{x^{\beta}}g)+\tilde{Q}_{\alpha\beta}(g;\partial g,\partial g)

for
\tilde{P}
and
\tilde{Q}_{\alpha\beta}
defined as in (
B.31
) and (
B.32
), respectively.

Now let
\chi:[0,\infty)\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a smooth cutoff function such that
\chi(\xi)=1

for
\xi\geq 3/4
,
\chi(\xi)=0
for
\xi\leq 1/2
and
0\leq\chi(\xi\textasciiacute)\leq 1
for all
\xi
.
For a solution
g
of the reduced Einstein-Vlasov system having ADM mass
M
, define
h^{0}
and
h^{1}
by

g=\eta+h=\eta+h^{0}+h^{1}\ ,\quad h^{0}_{\mu\nu}(x^{\alpha})=\chi\left(\frac{r}{1+x^{0}}\right)\frac{M}{r}\,\delta_{\mu\nu}\ ,

(D.4)

where
\eta
is the Minkowski metric; see
[
9
, (1.13)]
.
For fixed
0<\gamma<1
and
0<\mu<1-\gamma
, let also

w(x^{\alpha})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}(1+|r-x^{0}|)^{1+2\gamma}&\text{ if }&r>x^{0}\\
1+(1+|r-x^{0}|)^{-2\mu}&\text{ if }&r\leq x^{0}\end{array}\right..

The
energy
at time
x^{0}
(depending on a natural number
N
) is

E_{N}(x^{0})=\sum_{|I|\leq N}\lVert\sqrt{w(x^{0},\cdot)}\,\partial Z^{I}h^{1}(x^{0},\cdot)\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\ ,

where
I
is a multi-index and
Z^{I}
runs over all possible compositions of
|I|
of the vector fields

\Omega_{ij}=x^{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}-x^{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{i}}\ ,\quad B_{i}=x^{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}+x^{0}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{i}}\ ,\quad S=x^{0}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}+x^{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{i}}\ ,\quad\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\ .

(D.5)

Given an initial particle distribution
f_{0}=f_{0}(x^{i},p^{\beta})
at time
x^{0}=0
,
denote also, for a given
N
,

\mathcal{V}_{N}=\sum_{k+l\leq N}\lVert\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{p}^{l}f_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{p}}\ .

(D.6)

One of the main theorems in
[
9
]
, Theorem 1.2, together with estimate
[
9
, equ. (7.3)]

for the case
I=J=\emptyset
in their notation, can be quoted as follows:

Theorem 4.1
(
[
9
]
)

For any
0<\gamma<1
,
0<\mu<1-\gamma
,
0<\delta<\frac{1}{8}\min\{\gamma,1-\gamma\}
,

K>0
,
K^{\prime}>0
and
N\geq 11
, there are
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and constants
C_{N},C^{\prime}_{N}>0

such that, for all
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, given any initial data

(g_{\alpha\beta},\partial_{x^{0}}g_{\alpha\beta},f_{0})\big|_{x^{0}=0}

for the reduced Einstein-Vlasov system satisfying

E_{N}(0)^{1/2}+\mathcal{V}_{N}+M<\varepsilon

and

\mathrm{supp}(f_{0})\subset\{(x,p):|x|\leq K,|p|\leq K^{\prime}\},

there exists a corresponding global solution satisfying, for all
x^{0}\geq 0
,

\sqrt{E_{N}(x^{0})}+\sum_{|I|\leq N}(1+x^{0})\lVert Z^{I}T^{\mu\nu}(x^{0},\cdot)\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq C_{N}\varepsilon(1+x^{0})^{C_{N}^{\prime}\varepsilon}

(D.7)

along with the pointwise decay estimates

|Z^{I}h^{1}_{\mu\nu}(x^{\alpha})|\leq\frac{C^{\prime}_{N}\varepsilon(1+x^{0})^{C^{\prime}_{N}\varepsilon}}{(1+x^{0}+r)(1+(r-x^{0})_{+})^{\gamma}},\quad|I|\leq N-3,

and

|\partial h_{\mu\nu}(x^{\alpha})|\leq\frac{C^{\prime}_{N}\varepsilon(1+x^{0})^{2\delta}}{(1+x^{0}+r)(1+|r-x^{0}|){(1+(r-x^{0})_{+})}^{2\delta}},

where
(r-x^{0})_{+}
stands for
r-x^{0}
if
r\geq x^{0}
and 0 otherwise.

Estimate (
D.7
) immediately yields an
L^{2}
estimate for
T^{\mu\nu}
,
which we can transform into an
L^{\infty}
estimate using the Sobolev inequality

\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq D_{s}\lVert u\rVert_{W^{s,2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\quad\text{for all }u\in W^{s,2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\ ,

which is valid for
s>n/2
and where
D_{s}>0
is a universal constant
(we need
n=3
and
s=2
). By letting
N=11
in Theorem
4.1

and by only taking into account the standard derivatives among the vector fields (
D.5
),
we arrive at the following

Corollary 4.2

For any
0<\gamma<1
,
0<\mu<1-\gamma
,
0<\delta<\frac{1}{8}\min\{\gamma,1-\gamma\}
,

K>0
and
K^{\prime}>0
, there is
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and a constant
C_{0}>0
such that,
for all
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, given any initial data

(g_{\alpha\beta},\partial_{x^{0}}g_{\alpha\beta},f_{0})\big|_{x^{0}=0}

for the reduced Einstein-Vlasov system satisfying

E_{11}(0)^{1/2}+\mathcal{V}_{11}+M<\varepsilon

and

\mathrm{supp}(f_{0})\subset\{(x,p):|x|\leq K,|p|\leq K^{\prime}\},

there exists a corresponding global solution satisfying the following pointwise
decay estimates, for all
x^{0}\geq 0

and multi-indices
\kappa
with
|\kappa|\leq 8
,

\displaystyle|\partial^{\kappa}T^{\mu\nu}(x^{0},x)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{0}\varepsilon}{(1+x^{0})^{1-C_{0}\varepsilon}},

(D.8)

\displaystyle|\partial h_{\mu\nu}(x^{0},x)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{0}\varepsilon(1+x^{0})^{2\delta}}{(1+|x|+x^{0})(1+||x|-x^{0}|){(1+(|x|-x^{0})_{+})}^{2\delta}},

\displaystyle|\partial^{\kappa}(h^{1})_{\mu\nu}(x^{0},x)|

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{C_{0}\varepsilon(1+x^{0})^{C_{0}\varepsilon}}{(1+|x|+x^{0})(1+(|x|-x^{0})_{+})^{\gamma}}.

(D.9)

It should be noted that we do not make use of the smallness of

E_{11}(0)^{1/2}+\mathcal{V}_{11}
to derive the quadrupole formula;
this condition is only needed in order for the above corollary to hold.
Also note that estimates (
1.30
) and (
1.31
) in our boundedness
and decay conditions have been much relaxed as compared to what the above corollary gives.
However, there would have been no gain in our estimates had we used the
above better bound (
D.8
) for the energy-momentum tensor,
since such bounds only get applied at points satisfying
x^{0}={\cal O}(1)

and
t-|x|={\cal O}(1)
, for
x
in a ball of a fixed finite radius.

D.2
Estimates with factors of
c
and
G
included

We now translate the bounds from Corollary
4.2
into the framework of equations
that has been set up in our Section
1.1
. From now on
(x^{\alpha},p^{\beta})

denote the coordinates on the tangent bundle
T{\cal M}
as we used
in Section
1.1
. Our coordinates
(x^{0},x^{1},x^{2},x^{3})
have a dimension of
length
,
for which the constant
\lambda
represents a fundamental scale. The second half
(p^{0},p^{1},p^{2},p^{3})

of our canonical tangent-bundle coordinates have a dimension of
momentum
, which in terms
of our fundamental constants has units of
G^{-1}\lambda c^{3}
. Also let
m=G^{-1}\lambda c^{2}

be the Vlasov particles’ mass expressed in the correct physical units. Starting from this context,
we define new, dimensionless objects denoted with an overbar, which are themselves functions
of new dimensionless coordinates
\overline{x}
and
\overline{p}

on the tangent bundle:

\displaystyle\overline{x}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1}\ x^{\alpha}\ ,

\displaystyle\overline{p}^{\beta}

\displaystyle=m^{-1}c^{-1}\ p^{\beta}\ ,

\displaystyle\overline{g}_{\mu\nu}(\overline{x}^{\alpha})

\displaystyle=g_{\mu\nu}(\lambda\overline{x}^{\alpha})\ ,

\displaystyle\overline{T}^{\mu\nu}(\overline{x}^{\alpha})

\displaystyle=8\pi G\lambda^{2}c^{-4}\ T^{\mu\nu}(\lambda\overline{x}^{\alpha})\ ,

(D.10)

\displaystyle\overline{f}(\overline{x}^{\alpha},\overline{p}^{\beta})

\displaystyle=8\pi G\lambda^{2}c\ m^{5}\ f(\lambda\overline{x}^{\alpha},mc\overline{p}^{\beta})\ .

(D.11)

We check now that, if our objects satisfy our version of the Einstein-Vlasov system
as stated in Section
1.1
, then the equations of the Einstein-Vlasov system
as described in subsection
D.1
are valid for the objects with an overbar.
To begin, since Christoffel symbols involve taking first-order derivatives of the metric,
the Christoffel symbols of the overbar system are

\overline{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\mu\nu}(\overline{x}^{\alpha})=\lambda\Gamma^{\rho}_{\mu\nu}(\lambda\overline{x}^{\alpha})\ .

Likewise, the components of the Einstein tensor in the new coordinates are

\overline{G}_{\mu\nu}(\overline{x}^{\alpha})=\lambda^{2}G_{\mu\nu}(\lambda\overline{x}^{\alpha})\ .

Then the Einstein equation
G_{\alpha\beta}=8\pi Gc^{-4}T_{\alpha\beta}

from (
1.1
) implies

\overline{G}_{\mu\nu}(\overline{x}^{\alpha})=\lambda^{2}\,\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}\,T_{\mu\nu}(\lambda\overline{x}^{\alpha})=\overline{T}_{\mu\nu}(\overline{x}^{\alpha})\ ,

and thus the Einstein equation (
1.1
) as in subsection
D.1
is verified.
To check the validity of the Vlasov equation (
1.10
), let us abbreviate
the unimportant constant in front of
f
as
C=8\pi G\lambda^{2}cm^{5}
. Note the derivative relations

\frac{\partial\overline{f}}{\partial\overline{x}^{\mu}}(\overline{x}^{\alpha},\overline{p}^{\beta})=C\lambda\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{\mu}}(\lambda\overline{x}^{\alpha},mc\lambda\overline{p}^{\beta})\ ,\quad\frac{\partial\overline{f}}{\partial\overline{p}^{\mu}}(\overline{x}^{\alpha},\overline{p}^{\beta})=Cmc\frac{\partial f}{\partial p^{\mu}}(\lambda\overline{x}^{\alpha},mc\lambda\overline{p}^{\beta})\ .

Thus, from the assumption that our version of the Vlasov equation (
1.10
) holds:

p^{\mu}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{\mu}}(x^{\alpha},b^{\beta})-\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\sigma}\,p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial p^{\sigma}}(x^{\alpha},p^{\beta})=0,

we conclude that, at all points
(\overline{x}^{\alpha},\overline{p}^{\beta})
of the tangent bundle,

\displaystyle\overline{p}^{\mu}\frac{\partial\overline{f}}{\partial\overline{x}^{\mu}}(\overline{x}^{\alpha},\overline{p}^{\beta})-\overline{\Gamma}^{\sigma}_{\mu\nu}\,\overline{p}^{\mu}\overline{p}^{\nu}\frac{\partial\overline{f}}{\partial\overline{p}^{\sigma}}(\overline{x}^{\alpha},\overline{p}^{\beta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(mc)^{-1}p^{\mu}C\lambda\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{\mu}}(\lambda\overline{x}^{\alpha},mc\overline{p}^{\beta})-(mc)^{-2}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\lambda\,\Gamma^{\sigma}_{\mu\nu}Cmc\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial p^{\sigma}}(\lambda\overline{x}^{\alpha},mc\overline{p}^{\beta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C(mc)^{-1}\lambda\left(p^{\mu}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{\mu}}(x^{\alpha},p^{\beta})-\Gamma^{\sigma}_{\mu\nu}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\,\frac{\partial f}{\partial p^{\sigma}}(x^{\alpha},p^{\beta})\right)=0,

as needed. Finally we check the relation (
1.7
) between
T
and
f
. Consider
x=(x^{\alpha})
fixed
and denote by
\overline{x}=\lambda^{-1}x
the corresponding point in the new manifold coordinates.
We raise the indices and write the constant
m
where it belongs in our definition (
1.7
)
of
T_{\alpha\beta}
:

T^{\mu\nu}(x)=mc\,|g(x)|^{1/2}\int_{P{\cal M}(x)}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\,f(x,p^{\beta})\,\frac{dp^{1}\,dp^{2}\,dp^{3}}{-p_{0}}.

(D.12)

Then we perform the change of integration variables

\overline{p}^{i}=\frac{1}{mc}\,p^{i}\quad,\ i=1,2,3\ .

The
p_{0}=g_{\alpha 0}(x)p^{\alpha}
inside the integral is a function of
(p^{j})
defined
by the relation
g_{\alpha\beta}(x)\,p^{\alpha}p^{\beta}=-m^{2}c^{2}
from (
1.5
);
use it to define a new function
\overline{p}^{0}
of the new variables
\overline{p}^{j}
as

\overline{p}^{0}(\overline{p}^{j})=\frac{1}{mc}\,p^{0}\left(mc\overline{p}^{j}\right)\ .

Then the implicit relation that defines
\overline{p}^{0}
in terms of
\overline{p}^{j}
is

\overline{g}_{\mu\nu}(\overline{x})\,\overline{p}^{\mu}\overline{p}^{\nu}=g_{\mu\nu}(x)\,\overline{p}^{\mu}\overline{p}^{\nu}=-1\ .

(We also define the function
\overline{p}_{0}
by lowering the index from
\overline{p}^{0}
).
Under this change of variables, the domain of integration

P{\cal M}(x)=\{(p^{\nu})\in T_{x}{\cal M}\ :\ (p^{\nu})\text{ is future-directed, }g_{\alpha\beta}(x^{\mu})p^{\alpha}p^{\beta}=-m^{2}c^{2}\}

is mapped onto

\{(\overline{p}^{\nu})\in T_{\bar{x}}{\cal M}\ :\ (\overline{p}^{\nu})\text{ is future-directed, }\overline{g}_{\alpha\beta}(\overline{x})\overline{p}^{\alpha}\overline{p}^{\beta}=-1\}=:\overline{P{\cal M}}(\overline{x})\ ,

which is exactly the definition of the mass shell found in subsection
D.1
.
Hence the integral above for
T^{\mu\nu}(x)
, (
D.12
), becomes

\displaystyle T^{\mu\nu}(x)

\displaystyle=mc\,|g(x)|^{1/2}\int_{\overline{P{\cal M}}(\overline{x})}m^{2}c^{2}\overline{p}^{\mu}\overline{p}^{\nu}\,f(x,mc\overline{p}^{\beta})\frac{1}{-mc\overline{p}_{0}}m^{3}c^{3}\ \mathrm{d}\overline{p}^{1}\mathrm{d}\overline{p}^{2}\mathrm{d}\overline{p}^{3}

\displaystyle=m^{5}c^{5}\,|\det\overline{g}(\overline{x})|^{1/2}\int_{\overline{P{\cal M}}(\overline{x})}\overline{p}^{\mu}\overline{p}^{\nu}\,f(x,mc\overline{p}^{\beta})\,\frac{1}{-\overline{p}_{0}}\ \mathrm{d}\overline{p}^{1}\mathrm{d}\overline{p}^{2}\mathrm{d}\overline{p}^{3}\ .

Now we use the definition (
D.11
) of
\overline{f}
to turn this into

T^{\mu\nu}(x)=\frac{1}{8\pi}\,G^{-1}\lambda^{-2}c^{4}|\det\overline{g}(\overline{x})|^{1/2}\int_{\overline{P{\cal M}}(\overline{x})}\overline{p}^{\mu}\overline{p}^{\nu}\,\overline{f}(\overline{x},\overline{p})\,\frac{1}{-\overline{p}_{0}}\ \mathrm{d}\overline{p}^{1}\mathrm{d}\overline{p}^{2}\mathrm{d}\overline{p}^{3}\ .

Moving the constants to the left and using the definition (
D.10
) of
\overline{T}
,
we conclude that the energy-momentum-tensor formula (
1.7
) from subsection
D.1

is satisfied.

In particular, Corollary
4.2
applies to the objects with an overbar. To state what
it implies in terms of our original objects, note the relation between higher-order derivatives
of barred and unbarred quantities: For all multi-indices
\alpha
,
\beta
, we have

\displaystyle\partial^{\alpha}_{\bar{x}}\overline{T}^{\mu\nu}(\overline{x})

\displaystyle=8\pi G\lambda^{2+|\alpha|}c^{-4}\,\partial^{\alpha}_{x}T^{\mu\nu}(x)\ ,

\displaystyle\partial^{\alpha}_{\bar{x}}\overline{g}_{\mu\nu}(\overline{x})

\displaystyle=\lambda^{|\alpha|}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}g_{\mu\nu}(x)\ ,

\displaystyle\partial^{\alpha}_{\bar{x}}\partial^{\beta}_{\bar{p}}\overline{f}(\overline{x},\overline{p})

\displaystyle=8\pi G\lambda^{2+|\alpha|}c^{|\beta|}m^{5+|\beta|}\,\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\partial^{\beta}_{p}f(x,p)\ .

Also observe that, due to
\overline{r}=\lambda^{-1}r
and
\overline{M}=\frac{GM}{c^{2}\lambda}
,
the splitting of the metric (
D.4
) now translates into

g(x)=\eta+h^{0}(x)+h^{1}(x)\ ,\quad h^{0}_{\mu\nu}(x)=\chi\left(\frac{r}{\lambda+x^{0}}\right)\frac{GM}{c^{2}r}\,\delta_{\mu\nu}\ ,

(D.13)

where
\chi
is the same cutoff function used in (
D.4
). In particular,

h^{1}(x)=\overline{h^{1}}(\overline{x})
and the same for
h
. Therefore we obtain the following result
(with a modified constant
C_{0}
):

Corollary 4.3

For any
0<\gamma<1
,
0<\mu<1-\gamma
,
0<\delta<\frac{1}{8}\min\{\gamma,1-\gamma\}
,

K>0
and
K^{\prime}>0
, there is
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and a constant
C_{0}>0
such that,
for all
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, given any initial data

(g_{\alpha\beta},\partial_{x^{0}}g_{\alpha\beta},f_{0})\big|_{x^{0}=0}

for the Einstein-Vlasov system as in Section
1.1
so that

{\cal E}(0)+{\cal V}(0)+\frac{GM}{c^{2}\lambda}<\varepsilon

and

\mathrm{supp}(f_{0})\subset\{(x,p):|x|\leq\lambda K,\ |p|\leq mcK^{\prime}\},

there exists a corresponding global solution satisfying, for all
x^{0}\geq 0

and multi-indices
\kappa
with
|\kappa|\leq 8
,

|\partial^{\kappa}T^{\mu\nu}(x^{0},x)|\leq\frac{c^{4}}{G\lambda^{2+|\kappa|}}\,\frac{C_{0}\varepsilon}{(1+\lambda^{-1}x^{0})^{1-C_{0}\varepsilon}},

(D.14)

and furthermore the weak-field coefficients satisfy

|\partial h_{\mu\nu}(x^{0},x)|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\,\frac{C_{0}\varepsilon(1+\lambda^{-1}x^{0})^{2\delta}}{(1+\lambda^{-1}(|x|+x^{0}))(1+\lambda^{-1}||x|-x^{0}|){(1+\lambda^{-1}(|x|-x^{0})_{+})}^{2\delta}}

(D.15)

as well as

|\partial^{\kappa}(h^{1})_{\mu\nu}(x^{0},x)|\leq\dfrac{1}{\lambda^{|\kappa|}}\,\dfrac{C_{0}\varepsilon(1+\lambda^{-1}x^{0})^{C_{0}\varepsilon}}{(1+\lambda^{-1}(|x|+x^{0}))(1+\lambda^{-1}(|x|-x^{0})_{+})^{\gamma}}.

(D.16)

The quantities
{\cal E}(0)
and
{\cal V}(0)
are made explicit in Remark
4.4
(a) immediately below.

Remark 4.4

(a) We have

{\cal E}(0)=\overline{E}_{11}(0)
and
{\cal V}(0)=\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{11}

for the solution of the barred system resulting from the initial data

(\overline{g}_{\alpha\beta},\partial_{\overline{x}^{0}}\overline{g}_{\alpha\beta},\overline{f}_{0})\big|_{\overline{x}^{0}=0}
as obtained from

(g_{\alpha\beta},\partial_{x^{0}}g_{\alpha\beta},f_{0})\big|_{x^{0}=0}
.
Therefore, by (
D.6
) for
N=11
,

{\cal V}(0)=\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{11}=\sum_{k+l\leq 11}\|\partial^{k}_{\overline{x}}\,\partial^{l}_{\overline{p}}\,\overline{f}_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{\overline{x}}L^{2}_{\overline{p}}}.

Both
{\cal V}(0)
and
{\cal E}(0)
could now be expressed in the new variables,
but, since we will not need their explicit forms, we don’t expand this any further.

(b) All of the above inequalities for
T
and
h^{1}
concern only the time domain
x^{0}\geq 0
.
In our conditions in the introduction and in our estimates, however, the same inequalities
are needed also at negative values of time. We remark to this end that the exact same results
(possibly with a new constant
C_{0}
) still hold with
x^{0}
replaced by
|x^{0}|
, and the reason
for this is that one can evolve the initial data for the Einstein-Vlasov system backwards in time as well.

(c) Recall that in Section
1.1
we had (using the notation from there)

\tilde{T}_{\alpha\beta}(t,x^{a})=T_{\alpha\beta}(ct,x^{a})
, see (
1.11
),
and the former quantity was called
T
again. Hence, if we pass to the variables
(t,x^{a})

instead of
(x^{0},x^{a})=(ct,x^{a})
, then
x^{0}
has to be replaced by
ct
in (
D.14
),
and it has to be used that
\partial_{t}^{n}=c^{n}\partial_{x^{0}}^{n}
.

(d) Since
g
and its derivatives turn out to be uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}
,
the bounds in (
D.14
) for
|\partial^{\kappa}T^{\mu\nu}|
and in (
D.15
),
(
D.16
) for
|\partial h_{\mu\nu}|
,
|\partial^{\kappa}(h^{1})_{\mu\nu}|

could equally well be stated
for their index-raised/lowered counterparts
|\partial^{\kappa}T_{\mu\nu}|

and
|\partial h^{\mu\nu}|
,
|\partial^{\kappa}(h^{1})^{\mu\nu}|
, for a possibly different constant
C_{0}>0
.

Appendix E
Appendix: Some technical lemmas

This last section collects the statements and proofs of technical lemmas
that have been used in our estimates.

E.1
Bounds from our
a priori
conditions

Here we derive further bounds that follow from the conditions imposed in
Subsection
1.3
. Let
(g_{\alpha\beta},f)
be a solution
to the Einstein-Vlasov system satisfying those conditions for constants

\mathcal{T}
,
\mathcal{S}
,
\gamma
,
\delta
etc. as specified in the statement of Theorem
1.2
.

First we recall from (
1.17
) and (
1.29
) the splitting of the metric,

g(x)=\eta+h^{0}(x)+h^{1}(x),\quad h^{0}_{\mu\nu}(x)=\chi\left(\frac{r}{\lambda+|x_{0}|}\right)\frac{q}{\lambda^{-1}r}\,\delta_{\mu\nu},\quad x=(x^{\alpha}),

where
0\leq q\leq C\varepsilon_{h}\leq C\delta_{h}
by (
1.33
); the constants

\delta_{h}=C_{0}\varepsilon_{h}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\delta_{T,\,\mu\nu}^{\kappa}=C_{0}\varepsilon_{T,\,\mu\nu}^{\kappa},

(E.1)

as defined in Theorem
1.2
, will appear later, and, as in (
1.28
),

\chi:[0,\infty)\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}

is a smooth cutoff function such that
\chi(\xi)=1

for
\xi\geq 3/4
,
\chi(\xi)=0
for
\xi\leq 1/2
and
0\leq\chi(\xi\textasciiacute)\leq 1
for all
\xi
.

E.1.1
Bounds on
h^{0}

Due to the support properties of
\chi
we have

r\leq\frac{1}{2}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\,\,\Rightarrow\,\,\chi\Big(\frac{r}{\lambda+|x^{0}|}\Big)=\chi^{\prime}\Big(\frac{r}{\lambda+|x^{0}|}\Big)=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad r\geq\frac{3}{4}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\,\,\Rightarrow\,\,\chi^{\prime}\Big(\frac{r}{\lambda+|x^{0}|}\Big)=0.

(E.2)

If
\mu\neq\nu
, then
h^{0}_{\mu\nu}=0
.
Denoting
\xi=\frac{r}{\lambda+|x^{0}|}
, we calculate for
x^{0}\neq 0

and equal indices

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{j}}h^{0}_{\mu\mu}=-\lambda q\chi(\xi)\,\frac{x^{j}}{|x|^{3}}+\lambda q\chi^{\prime}(\xi)\,\frac{1}{\lambda+|x^{0}|}\,\frac{x^{j}}{|x|^{2}},\quad\partial_{x^{0}}h^{0}_{\mu\mu}=-\lambda q\chi^{\prime}(\xi)\frac{1}{(\lambda+|x^{0}|)^{2}}\frac{x_{0}}{|x_{0}|},

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{k}}\partial_{x^{j}}h^{0}_{\mu\mu}=-3\lambda q\chi^{\prime}(\xi)\frac{1}{\lambda+|x^{0}|}\frac{x^{k}x^{j}}{|x|^{4}}-\lambda q\chi(\xi)\frac{\delta^{jk}}{|x|^{3}}+3\lambda q\chi(\xi)\frac{x^{k}x^{j}}{|x|^{5}}

\displaystyle\hskip 58.0001pt+\,\lambda q\chi^{\prime\prime}(\xi)\frac{1}{(\lambda+|x^{0}|)^{2}}\frac{x^{k}x^{j}}{|x|^{3}}+\lambda q\chi^{\prime}(\xi)\frac{1}{\lambda+|x^{0}|}\frac{\delta^{jk}}{|x|^{2}},

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{0}}\partial_{x^{j}}h^{0}_{\mu\mu}=-\lambda q\chi^{\prime\prime}(\xi)\frac{1}{(\lambda+|x^{0}|)^{3}}\frac{x^{0}}{|x^{0}|}\frac{x^{j}}{|x|},

\displaystyle\pm\partial_{x^{0}}^{2}h^{0}_{\mu\mu}=\lambda q\chi^{\prime\prime}(\xi)\frac{|x|}{(\lambda+|x^{0}|)^{4}}+2\lambda q\chi^{\prime}(\xi)\frac{1}{(\lambda+|x^{0}|)^{3}}\frac{x^{0}}{|x^{0}|},

and similarly for the third-order and fourth-order derivatives.
Hence for
\lambda=0,1,2,3
, also using (
E.2
),

\displaystyle|\partial_{x^{\lambda}}h^{0}_{\mu\mu}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\lambda\delta_{h}|\chi^{\prime}(\xi)|\frac{1}{(\lambda+|x^{0}|)^{2}}+C\lambda\delta_{h}\chi(\xi)\,\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}+C\lambda\delta_{h}|\chi^{\prime}(\xi)|\,\frac{1}{\lambda+|x^{0}|}\,\frac{1}{|x|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\lambda\delta_{h}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\}}\,\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}+C\lambda\delta_{h}\,{\bf 1}_{\{\frac{1}{2}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\leq r\leq\frac{3}{4}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\}}\,\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda+|x^{0}|}\,\frac{1}{|x|}+\frac{1}{(\lambda+|x^{0}|)^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\lambda\delta_{h}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\}}\,\frac{1}{|x|^{2}};

the last estimate is due to
||x^{0}|-|x||=|ct-|x||\leq\lambda
, which implies

\lambda+|x^{0}|\geq|x|=r
. In an analogous manner it is verified that

|\partial^{\alpha}h^{0}_{\mu\mu}|\leq C\lambda\delta_{h}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\}}\,\frac{1}{|x|^{3}},\quad|\alpha|=2,

and

|\partial^{\alpha}h^{0}_{\mu\mu}|\leq\lambda C\delta_{h}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\}}\,\frac{1}{|x|^{4}},\quad|\alpha|=3,

so that we get

|\partial^{\alpha}h^{0}_{\mu\nu}|\leq C\lambda\delta_{h}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\}}\,\frac{1}{|x|^{1+|\alpha|}},\quad|\alpha|\leq 3.

(E.3)

Therefore we can also state the following bounds for later use:

\displaystyle|(\partial_{x^{\alpha}}h^{0}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{x^{\beta}}h^{0}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}})(x^{0},x)|\leq C\lambda^{2}\delta_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\}}\,\frac{1}{|x|^{4}},

(E.4)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}(\partial_{x^{\beta}}h^{0}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{x^{\gamma}}h^{0}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}})(x^{0},x)|\leq C\lambda^{2}\delta_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\}}\,\frac{1}{|x|^{5}},

(E.5)

\displaystyle|(h^{0}_{\mu\nu}\partial^{2}_{x^{\alpha}x^{\beta}}h^{0}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}})(x^{0},x)|\leq C\lambda^{2}\delta_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\}}\,\frac{1}{|x|^{4}},

(E.6)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}(h^{0}_{\mu\nu}\partial^{2}_{x^{\beta}x^{\gamma}}h^{0}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}})(x^{0},x)|\leq C\lambda^{2}\delta_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\}}\,\frac{1}{|x|^{5}}.

(E.7)

E.1.2
Bounds on
h^{1}

Due to the decay condition (
1.32
) we have

|\partial^{\kappa}h^{1}_{\mu\nu}(x^{0},x)|\leq C\lambda^{-|\kappa|}\delta_{h}\,\frac{(1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|)^{\delta_{h}}}{{(1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|+\lambda^{-1}|x|)(1+\lambda^{-1}(|x|-|x^{0}|)_{+})}^{\gamma_{0}}}

(E.8)

for
|\kappa|\leq 4
and with
\delta_{h}=C_{0}\varepsilon_{h}
. In particular,

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}|(\partial_{x^{\alpha}}h^{1}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{x^{\beta}}h^{1}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}})(x^{0},x)|+\lambda^{3}|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}(\partial_{x^{\beta}}h^{1}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{x^{\gamma}}h^{1}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}})(x^{0},x)|

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\leq\,\,C\delta_{h}^{2}\,\frac{(1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|)^{2\delta_{h}}}{{(1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|+\lambda^{-1}|x|)^{2}(1+\lambda^{-1}(|x|-|x^{0}|)_{+})}^{2\gamma_{0}}},\qquad

(E.9)

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}|(h^{1}_{\mu\nu}\partial^{2}_{x^{\alpha}x^{\beta}}h^{1}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}})(x^{0},x)|+\lambda^{3}|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}(h^{1}_{\mu\nu}\partial^{2}_{x^{\beta}x^{\gamma}}h^{1}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}})(x^{0},x)|

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\leq\,\,C\delta_{h}^{2}\frac{(1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|)^{2\delta_{h}}}{{(1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|+\lambda^{-1}|x|)^{2}(1+\lambda^{-1}(|x|-|x^{0}|)_{+})}^{2\gamma_{0}}},

(E.10)

is verified. Note that there is no gain for
h^{1}_{\mu\nu}

if one takes a derivative, and further that the term
{(1+\lambda^{-1}(|x|-|x^{0}|)_{+})}^{\gamma_{0}}

will not help for
||x^{0}|-|x||\leq\lambda
.

E.1.3
Bounds on combinations of
h^{0}
and
h^{1}

Combining (
E.3
) with (
E.8
),
we get in particular the following estimates:

\displaystyle\Big(\lambda|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}h^{0}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{x^{\beta}}h^{1}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}|+\lambda^{2}|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}(\partial_{x^{\beta}}h^{0}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{x^{\gamma}}h^{1}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}})|\Big)(x^{0},x)

(E.11)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\}}\,\frac{(1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|)^{\delta_{h}}}{(1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|+\lambda^{-1}|x|){(1+\lambda^{-1}(|x|-|x^{0}|)_{+})}^{\gamma_{0}}}\,\frac{1}{|x|},

\displaystyle\Big(\lambda|h^{0}_{\mu\nu}\partial^{2}_{x^{\alpha}x^{\beta}}h^{1}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}|+\lambda^{2}|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}(h^{0}_{\mu\nu}\partial^{2}_{x^{\beta}x^{\gamma}}h^{1}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}})|\Big)(x^{0},x)

(E.12)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\}}\,\frac{(1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|)^{\delta_{h}}}{(1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|+\lambda^{-1}|x|){(1+\lambda^{-1}(|x|-|x^{0}|)_{+})}^{\gamma_{0}}}\,\frac{1}{|x|},

\displaystyle\Big(\lambda|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}h^{1}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{x^{\beta}}h^{0}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}|+\lambda^{2}|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}(\partial_{x^{\beta}}h^{1}_{\mu\nu}\partial_{x^{\gamma}}h^{0}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}})|\Big)(x^{0},x)

(E.13)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\}}\,\frac{(1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|)^{\delta_{h}}}{(1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|+\lambda^{-1}|x|){(1+\lambda^{-1}(|x|-|x^{0}|)_{+})}^{\gamma_{0}}}\,\frac{1}{|x|},

\displaystyle\Big(\lambda|h^{1}_{\mu\nu}\partial^{2}_{x^{\alpha}x^{\beta}}h^{0}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}|+\lambda^{2}|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}(h^{1}_{\mu\nu}\partial^{2}_{x^{\beta}x^{\gamma}}h^{0}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}})|\Big)(x^{0},x)

(E.14)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta_{h}^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(\lambda+|x^{0}|)\}}\,\frac{(1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|)^{\delta_{h}}}{(1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|+\lambda^{-1}|x|){(1+\lambda^{-1}(|x|-|x^{0}|)_{+})}^{\gamma_{0}}}\,\frac{1}{|x|}.

Also, for
h=h^{0}+h^{1}
we have

\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 4}\lambda^{|\alpha|}|\partial^{\alpha}h_{\mu\nu}(x^{0},x)|\leq C\delta_{h},

(E.15)

which in turn yields

\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 4}\lambda^{|\alpha|}|\partial^{\alpha}g_{\mu\nu}(x^{0},x)|\leq C.

(E.16)

In addition,

|h_{\mu\nu}(x^{0},x)|+\lambda|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}h_{\mu\nu}(x^{0},x)|\leq C\delta_{h}\,\frac{(1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|)^{\delta_{h}}}{1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|+\lambda^{-1}|x|};

(E.17)

this follows from (
E.3
) and (
E.8
).

E.1.4
Bounds on
H

Consider the matrix equations for the metric and its inverse:

(g_{\alpha\beta})=\hat{g}=\hat{m}+\hat{h}=(\eta_{\alpha\beta})+(h_{\alpha\beta})\ ,\quad(g^{\alpha\beta})=\hat{g}^{-1}=\hat{m}^{-1}+\hat{H}=(\eta^{\alpha\beta})+(H^{\alpha\beta})\ .

(E.18)

If
\varepsilon_{h}>0
(and hence
\delta_{h}>0
) is sufficiently small,
then (
E.15
) in particular yields
|\hat{h}|\leq 1/2
,
which shows that the inverse
(g^{\alpha\beta})
exists.
We can find
\hat{H}
in terms of the other matrices above as follows:

I=(\hat{m}+\hat{h})(\hat{m}^{-1}+\hat{H})=I+\hat{h}\hat{m}^{-1}+\hat{g}\hat{H},

which implies that

\hat{H}=-\hat{g}^{-1}\hat{h}\hat{m}^{-1}=-(\hat{m}+\hat{h})^{-1}\hat{h}\hat{m}^{-1}=-(I+\hat{m}^{-1}\hat{h})^{-1}\hat{m}^{-1}\hat{h}\hat{m}^{-1}\ .

Define

Z=\hat{H}\hat{m}\ ,\quad X=\hat{m}^{-1}\hat{h}\ ,\quad Y=(I+X)^{-1}\ .

Then we have the relations

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle=-YX\ ,

\displaystyle\dot{Z}

\displaystyle=-\dot{Y}X-Y\dot{X}\ ,

\displaystyle\ddot{Z}

\displaystyle=-\ddot{Y}X-\dot{Y}\dot{X}-Y\ddot{X}\ ,

\displaystyle\dddot{Z}

\displaystyle=-\dddot{Y}X-\ddot{Y}\dot{X}-\dot{Y}\ddot{X}-Y\dddot{X}\ ,

where a dot represents a derivative
\partial_{x^{\gamma}}
for an arbitrary
\gamma=0,1,2,3
.
Note that these formulas for higher-order derivatives only work if all derivatives
are with respect to the same index
\gamma
; if differentiating with respect to different indices,
the formulas would be longer. However, the natural sup bound obtained from them
would be the same as the one we will obtain now for the formulas above, so there is
no loss in doing this calculation for one fixed
\gamma
only.

From
Y(I+X)=I
the derivative of
Y
is found to be
\dot{Y}=-Y\dot{X}Y
.
This in turn yields

\displaystyle\ddot{Y}

\displaystyle=-\dot{Y}\dot{X}Y-Y\ddot{X}Y-Y\dot{X}\dot{Y}

\displaystyle=Y\dot{X}Y\dot{X}Y-Y\ddot{X}Y+Y\dot{X}Y\dot{X}Y

\displaystyle=Y\big(2\dot{X}Y\dot{X}-\ddot{X}\big)Y

and

\displaystyle\dddot{Y}

\displaystyle=\dot{Y}\big(2\dot{X}Y\dot{X}-\ddot{X}\big)Y+Y\big(2\ddot{X}Y\dot{X}+2\dot{X}\dot{Y}\dot{X}+2\dot{X}Y\ddot{X}-\dddot{X}\big)Y+Y\big(2\dot{X}Y\dot{X}-\ddot{X}\big)\dot{Y}

\displaystyle=-Y\dot{X}Y(2\dot{X}Y\dot{X}-\ddot{X})Y+Y\big(2\ddot{X}Y\dot{X}-2\dot{X}Y\dot{X}Y\dot{X}+2\dot{X}Y\ddot{X}-\dddot{X}\big)Y-Y\big(2\dot{X}Y\dot{X}-\ddot{X}\big)Y\dot{X}Y

\displaystyle=Y\bigg(-\dot{X}Y(2\dot{X}Y\dot{X}-\ddot{X})+\big(2\ddot{X}Y\dot{X}-2\dot{X}Y\dot{X}Y\dot{X}+2\dot{X}Y\ddot{X}-\dddot{X}\big)-\big(2\dot{X}Y\dot{X}-\ddot{X}\big)Y\dot{X}\bigg)Y.

From (
E.15
) and the constancy of
\hat{m}^{-1}
we get

\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}\lambda^{|\alpha|}|\partial^{\alpha}X|\leq C\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}\lambda^{|\alpha|}|\partial^{\alpha}\hat{h}|\leq C\delta_{h}.

As for
|Y|
,

|Y|=|(I+X)^{-1}|=\Big|\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{j}X^{j}\Big|\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|X|^{j}=\frac{1}{1-|X|}\leq 2,

since we may assume that
|X|\leq C\delta_{h}\leq 1/2
.
Therefore, it follows from the above formulas that

\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq 3}\lambda^{|\alpha|}|\partial^{\alpha}Y|\leq C\delta_{h}.

Going back to the derivatives of
Z
in terms of
X
and
Y
, we deduce

\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}\lambda^{|\alpha|}|\partial^{\alpha}Z|\leq C\delta_{h}.

Finally, note that these same bounds apply for the matrix

\hat{H}=Z\hat{m}^{-1}
. As a consequence,

\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}\lambda^{|\alpha|}|\partial^{\alpha}H^{\mu\nu}|\leq C\delta_{h}.

(E.19)

E.1.5
Supplementary bounds

Since
(g^{\alpha\beta})=(\eta^{\alpha\beta})+(H^{\alpha\beta})
by (
E.18
),
it follows from (
E.19
) that

\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}\lambda^{|\alpha|}|\partial^{\alpha}g^{\mu\nu}|\leq C.

(E.20)

For the Christoffel symbols,

\Gamma^{\sigma}_{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{2}\,g^{\sigma\kappa}\,(\partial_{x^{\mu}}g_{\nu\kappa}+\partial_{x^{\nu}}g_{\mu\kappa}-\partial_{x^{\kappa}}g_{\mu\nu})=\frac{1}{2}\,g^{\sigma\kappa}\,(\partial_{x^{\mu}}h_{\nu\kappa}+\partial_{x^{\nu}}h_{\mu\kappa}-\partial_{x^{\kappa}}h_{\mu\nu}),

(E.21)

the last estimate and (
E.15
) leads to

\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\lambda^{|\alpha|+1}|\partial^{\alpha}\Gamma^{\sigma}_{\mu\nu}|\leq C\delta_{h}.

From (
E.21
), (
E.20
),
h=h^{0}+h^{1}
and the decay condition (
1.30
) we also find

\lambda\,|\Gamma^{\sigma}_{\mu\nu}(t,x)|\leq\frac{C\delta_{h}}{{(1+\lambda^{-1}|x^{0}|)}^{1-2\delta}(1+\lambda^{-1}||x|-|x^{0}||)}.

(E.22)

As a reminder, the bounds in (
1.30
) are

\lambda^{|\kappa|}\,|\partial^{\kappa}T_{\mu\nu}(x^{0},x)|\leq C\frac{c^{4}}{G\lambda^{2}}\,\delta_{T,\,\mu\nu}^{\kappa}\quad\mbox{for}\quad|\kappa|\leq 4,\,\,(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{4}.

Due to

T^{\mu\nu}=g^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}g^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}T_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}

and (
E.20
), this yields

\lambda^{|\kappa|}\,|\partial^{\kappa}T^{\mu\nu}(x^{0},x)|\leq C\frac{c^{4}}{G\lambda^{2}}\,\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{|\kappa|}\delta_{T}^{(j)}\Big)\quad\mbox{for}\quad|\kappa|\leq 4,\,\,(x^{0},x)\in\mathbb{R}^{4},

(E.23)

where

\delta_{T}^{(j)}=\max\{\delta_{T,\,\mu\nu}^{\kappa}:|\kappa|=j,\mu,\nu=0,1,2,3\}.

E.1.6
Bounds on the support of
f
and
T_{\mu\nu}

Here we denote by
f(t)
the function
f(t,\cdot,\cdot)
, where
t
is fixed. The Vlasov equation

c^{-1}\partial_{t}f+\frac{p^{a}}{p^{0}}\,\partial_{x^{a}}f-\frac{1}{p^{0}}\,\Gamma_{\beta\gamma}^{a}\,p^{\beta}p^{\gamma}\,\partial_{p^{a}}f=0

from (
1.2
) with
p^{0}>0
being defined by the mass-shell condition

g_{\alpha\beta}\,p^{\alpha}p^{\beta}=-c^{2}
implies that

\frac{d}{dt}f(t,x^{a}(t),p^{b}(t))=0

for the solutions
(x^{a}(t),p^{b}(t))
of the characteristic equations

\frac{dx^{a}}{dt}=\frac{c}{p^{0}}\,p^{a},\quad\frac{dp^{b}}{dt}=-\frac{c}{p^{0}}\,\Gamma_{\beta\gamma}^{b}\,p^{\beta}p^{\gamma},

(E.24)

see (
1.8
) and (
1.9
). As observed in
[
9
, Prop. 2.1]
,
which we basically follow here, the main issue to control
\mathrm{supp}(f(t))

is to show that after a finite time
the characteristic has to enter the “far zone” where
cs-|X(s)|\succsim cs
,
which makes it possible to make use of the additional factor in the denominator
of (
E.22
). On the mass shell we have

|-(p^{0})^{2}+|p|^{2}+c^{2}|=|(\eta_{\alpha\beta}-g_{\alpha\beta})p^{\alpha}p^{\beta}|\leq C\delta_{h}\sum_{\alpha\beta}|p^{\alpha}p^{\beta}|\leq C\delta_{h}((p^{0})^{2}+|p|^{2})

(E.25)

by (
E.15
) and Hölder’s inequality. Thus

\frac{|p|^{2}}{(p^{0})^{2}}\leq C_{h}\delta\Big(1+\frac{|p|^{2}}{(p^{0})^{2}}\Big)+1-\frac{c^{2}}{(p^{0})^{2}}

and hence

\frac{|p|^{2}}{(p^{0})^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{1-C\delta_{h}}\Big(C\delta_{h}+1-\frac{c^{2}}{(p^{0})^{2}}\Big)

(E.26)

for
\delta_{h}\sim\varepsilon_{h}
, cf. (
E.1
), sufficiently small.
But (
E.25
) also yields
(p^{0})^{2}\leq C\delta_{h}((p^{0})^{2}+|p|^{2})+|p|^{2}+c^{2}
, so that

(p^{0})^{2}\leq 4|p|^{2}+2c^{2}

(E.27)

for
\delta_{h}
small enough.

For
K^{\prime}_{0}>0
appearing in the compact-support condition (
1.24
), we may assume that
K^{\prime}_{0}\geq 2
.

Lemma 5.1

Under the assumption

\mathrm{supp}(f(0))\subset\{(x,p):|x|\leq\lambda K_{0},\ |p|\leq cK^{\prime}_{0}\},

let
\beta_{0}\in(0,1)
be defined through

1-\beta_{0}^{2}=\frac{1}{4(8K^{\prime}_{0}+1)}.

If
\varepsilon>0
is small enough, then

\mathrm{supp}(f(t))\subset\Big\{(x,p):|x|\leq\lambda K_{0}+\beta_{0}c|t|,\ |p|\leq c(K^{\prime}_{0}+1)\Big\},\quad t\in\mathbb{R}.

Proof :
We consider non-negative
t
only.
If
(x,p)\in\mathrm{supp}(f(t))
,
then
(x,p)=(X(t),P(t))
for a unique characteristic
such that
(\tilde{x},\tilde{p})=(X(0),P(0))\in\mathrm{supp}(f_{0})
.
Therefore
|\tilde{x}|\leq\lambda K_{0}
and
|\tilde{p}|\leq cK^{\prime}_{0}
by the compact-support condition (
1.24
).
Let
S\in]0,\infty]
denote the largest time such that
|P(s)|\leq 2cK^{\prime}_{0}

for all
s\in[0,S)
.
Since the characteristic stays on the mass shell, (
E.27
) yields

(P^{0}(s))^{2}\leq 4|P(s)|^{2}+2c^{2}\leq(16K^{\prime}_{0}+2)c^{2}=\frac{c^{2}}{2(1-\beta_{0}^{2})},\quad s\in[0,S).

Thus we have

(P^{0}(s))^{2}\leq\frac{c^{2}}{C\delta_{h}(1+\beta_{0}^{2})+1-\beta_{0}^{2}},\quad s\in[0,S),

if
\delta_{h}\sim\varepsilon_{h}
is sufficiently small.
From (
E.26
) we hence obtain

\frac{|P(s)|^{2}}{(P^{0}(s))^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{1-C\delta_{h}}\Big(C\delta_{h}+1-\frac{c^{2}}{(P^{0}(s))^{2}}\Big)\leq\beta_{0}^{2},\quad s\in[0,S).

(E.28)

Owing to (
E.24
) this leads to

\frac{d}{ds}\,(cs-|X(s)|)=c-c\,\frac{X(s)}{|X(s)|}\cdot\frac{P(s)}{P^{0}(s)}\geq(1-\beta_{0})c,\quad s\in[0,S),

and it follows that

cs-|X(s)|\geq-|\tilde{x}|+(1-\beta_{0})cs\geq(1-\beta_{0})cs-K_{0},\quad s\in[0,S).

(E.29)

From the second equation in (
E.24
) and (
E.22
) we deduce

\displaystyle|P(s)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\tilde{p}|+\int_{0}^{s}|\dot{P}(\tau)|\,d\tau\leq cK^{\prime}_{0}+c\int_{0}^{s}\frac{1}{P^{0}(\tau)}\,|\Gamma^{\cdot}_{\mu\nu}(\tau,X(\tau))\,P^{\mu}(\tau)P^{\nu}(\tau)|\,d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle cK^{\prime}_{0}+2c^{2}\,C\beta_{0}K^{\prime}_{0}\delta_{h}\int_{0}^{s}\,\frac{d\tau}{{(1+c\tau)}^{1-2\delta}(1+|c\tau-|X(\tau)||)}.

As a consequence, if
S\leq\frac{K_{0}}{(1-\beta_{0})c}
, then

|P(s)|\leq cK^{\prime}_{0}+c^{2}\,C\delta_{h}\int_{0}^{\frac{K_{0}}{(1-\beta_{0})c}}\,\frac{d\tau}{{(1+c\tau)}^{1-2\delta}}\leq cK^{\prime}_{0}+c\,\frac{C\delta_{h}}{\delta}\Big(1+\frac{K_{0}}{1-\beta_{0}}\Big)^{2\delta}\leq c(K^{\prime}_{0}+1),\quad s\in[0,S),

if
\delta_{h}\sim\varepsilon_{h}
is small enough. On the other hand,
if
S\geq\frac{K_{0}}{(1-\beta_{0})c}
, then using (
E.29
)

\displaystyle|P(s)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle cK^{\prime}_{0}+c\,C\delta_{h}+c^{2}\,C\delta_{h}\int_{\frac{K_{0}}{(1-\beta_{0})c}}^{\infty}\,\frac{d\tau}{{(1+c\tau)}^{1-2\delta}(1+(1-\beta_{0})c\tau-K_{0})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle cK^{\prime}_{0}+c\,C\delta_{h}+c\,C\delta_{h}\int_{\frac{K_{0}}{1-\beta_{0}}}^{\infty}\,\frac{d\sigma}{{(1+\sigma)}^{1-2\delta}(1+(1-\beta_{0})\sigma-K_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle cK^{\prime}_{0}+c\,C\delta_{h},\quad s\in[0,S),

since
1-2\delta+1=2-2\delta>1
(recall that
\delta<\frac{1}{2}
). Therefore if
\delta_{h}\sim\varepsilon_{h}

is sufficiently small, then we can achieve the bound
|P(s)|\leq c(K^{\prime}_{0}+1)
for
s\in[0,S)
in both cases.
Observing that
K^{\prime}_{0}+1\leq 2K^{\prime}_{0}-1
, the definition of
S
shows that we must have
S=\infty
, and in particular
we get
|P(s)|\leq c(K^{\prime}_{0}+1)
for all
s\in[0,\infty)
. Furthermore, due to (
E.24
) and (
E.28
) we find that

|X(s)|\leq|\tilde{x}|+c\int_{0}^{s}\frac{|P(\tau)|}{P^{0}(\tau)}\,d\tau\leq\lambda K_{0}+c\beta_{0}s,\quad s\in[0,\infty),

and this completes the argument.

\Box

Corollary 5.2

Let
u=t-c^{-1}r
for
r=|x|
and put

K_{\ast}=\frac{K_{0}+\beta_{0}}{1-\beta_{0}}.

(a)

If
|\bar{x}|\geq\lambda K_{\ast}
and
|u|\leq\lambda c^{-1}
,
then
T_{\alpha\beta}(t-c^{-1}|x-\bar{x}|,\bar{x})=T^{\alpha\beta}(t-c^{-1}|x-\bar{x}|,\bar{x})=0
.

(b)

If
|x|\geq\lambda K_{\ast}
and
|u|\leq\lambda c^{-1}
,
then
T_{\alpha\beta}(t,x)=T^{\alpha\beta}(t,x)=0
.

The same is true for derivatives of
T_{\alpha\beta}
or
T^{\alpha\beta}
.

Proof :
(a) From (
1.7
), more precisely (
1.11
),
we obtain

T^{\alpha\beta}(t,x)=c\,|g(t,x)|^{1/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}p^{\alpha}p^{\beta}\,f(t,x,p)\,\frac{dp}{-p_{0}}.

If
|\bar{x}|\geq\lambda K_{\ast}
and
|u|\leq\lambda c^{-1}
, then

\lambda K_{0}+\beta_{0}c(t-c^{-1}|x-\bar{x}|)=\lambda K_{0}+\beta_{0}cu+\beta_{0}|x|-\beta_{0}|x-\bar{x}|\leq\lambda K_{0}+\lambda\beta_{0}+\beta_{0}|\bar{x}|\leq|\bar{x}|.

Hence the claim follows from Lemma
5.1
.
(b) This is shown in the same way.

\Box

E.2
Bounds for some integrals

Here we prove general bounds regarding integrals of the type appearing in Lemma (
3.1
).
As a preparation, for suitable functions
\psi
and
\tilde{\psi}
we wish to consider
\partial_{x^{\alpha}}I
, where

I(t,x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy,\quad\alpha,\lambda=0,1,2,3.

We split up the integral
I=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}
according to

\displaystyle I_{1}(t,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|y|\leq{\cal S}}\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy,

\displaystyle I_{2}(t,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|x-y|\geq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy,

\displaystyle I_{3}(t,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy,

and study these integrals separately, under various assumptions
on
\psi
and
\tilde{\psi}
. We will assume throughout this section that

|t-|x||\leq 1,\quad|x|\geq\max\{12,{\cal T},2{\cal S}\},\quad{\cal T}\geq 2,\quad{\cal S}\geq\max\{2{\cal T},6\}.

For conciseness, no physical constants such as
c
or
\lambda
are included.
In addition,
\rho>0
will simply be a parameter that is carried along.

E.2.1
I_{3}

First we are going to deal with

\partial_{x^{\alpha}}I_{3},\quad I_{3}(t,x)=\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy,\quad\alpha,\lambda=0,1,2,3

Note that

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|z|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|x+z|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|z|,x+z)}{|z|}\,dz

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t+{\cal T}}d\sigma\int_{|z|=\sigma}dS(z)\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-\sigma,x+z)}{\sigma}.

This yields

\displaystyle\partial_{t}I_{3}(t,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}dS(z)\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(-{\cal T},x+z)}{t+{\cal T}}

(E.30)

\displaystyle+\,\int_{0}^{t+{\cal T}}d\sigma\int_{|z|=\sigma}dS(z)\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}\frac{\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-\sigma,x+z)}{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}dS(z)\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(-{\cal T},x+z)

\displaystyle+\,\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy.

For the
x^{k}
-derivatives of
I_{3}
,
k=1,2,3
, Lemma
5.10
implies that

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3}(t,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}dS(z)\,\frac{z_{k}}{|z|}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\leq{\cal S}\}}\,(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(-{\cal T},x+z)

\displaystyle+\,\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T}}\frac{\partial_{x^{k}}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy

\displaystyle+\,\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},|y|\leq{\cal S}}\bigg(\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}+\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)\bigg)\,\frac{x_{k}-y_{k}}{|x-y|^{2}}\,dy

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,1}(t,x)+\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,2}(t,x)+\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,3}(t,x).

We consider three sets of assumptions (cases I-III in what follows) to bound these integrals.

Case I.
We assume that

\displaystyle|(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t,x)|\leq C\rho^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(1+|t|)\}}\,\frac{1}{|x|^{3}},\quad\lambda=0,1,2,3.

(E.31)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t,x)|\leq C\rho^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(1+|t|)\}}\,\frac{1}{|x|^{4}},\quad\alpha,\lambda=0,1,2,3.

(E.32)

From (
E.30
) we recall that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}I_{3}(t,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}dS(z)\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(-{\cal T},x+z)

\displaystyle+\,\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy.

For the boundary term, as a consequence of (
E.31
), we get

\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}dS(z)\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}|(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(-{\cal T},x+z)|\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}\frac{dS(z)}{|x+z|^{3}}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|},

where we have used Lemma
5.6
for
\sigma=0
in the last estimate.
For the volume term, owing to (
E.32
) and Lemma
5.11
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{|\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)|}{|x-y|}\,dy

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}{\bf 1}_{\{|y|\geq\frac{1}{2}(1+|t-|x-y||)\}}\,\frac{dy}{|x-y||y|^{4}}\leq C(1+{\cal T})\,\frac{\rho^{2}}{|x|}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}.

Thus we have shown that

|\partial_{t}I_{3}|\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}.

(E.33)

For the
x^{k}
-derivatives, we first consider

\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,1}=-\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}dS(z)\,\frac{z_{k}}{|z|}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\leq{\cal S}\}}\,(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(-{\cal T},x+z).

Then (
E.31
) yields

|\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,1}|\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}\frac{dS(z)}{|x+z|^{3}}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}.

Next, for

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T}}\frac{\partial_{x^{k}}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy

we can use the bound (
E.32
). Therefore we find, as above,

|\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,2}|\leq C\rho^{2}\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T}}{\bf 1}_{\{|y|\geq\frac{1}{2}(1+|t-|x-y||)\}}\,\frac{dy}{|x-y||y|^{4}}\leq C\rho^{2}(1+{\cal T})\,\frac{1}{|x|}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|},

by means of Lemma
5.11
.

Lastly,

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},|y|\leq{\cal S}}\bigg(\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}+\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)\bigg)\,\frac{x_{k}-y_{k}}{|x-y|^{2}}\,dy.

If
|y|\leq{\cal S}
, then
|x-y|\geq|x|-|y|\geq|x|/2
. In addition,

|\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi}|\leq C\rho^{2}
by (
E.31
)
and
|\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})|\leq C\rho^{2}
by (
E.32
).
Therefore

\displaystyle|\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,3}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\int_{|y|\leq{\cal S}}\bigg(\frac{1}{|x-y|}+1\bigg)\,\frac{1}{|x-y|}\,dy\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}.

So if we summarize the bounds on
I_{3}
, we have shown
that (
E.31
)-(
E.32
) implies that

|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}I_{3}|\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|},\quad\alpha=0,1,2,3.

(E.34)

Case II.
We assume that

|(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t,x)|+|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t,x)|\leq C\rho^{2}\frac{(1+|t|)^{2\delta}}{(1+|t|+|x|)^{2}(1+(|x|-|t|)_{+})^{2\gamma}},\quad\alpha,\lambda=0,1,2,3.

(E.35)

By (
E.30
) we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}I_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}dS(z)\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(-{\cal T},x+z)

(E.36)

\displaystyle+\,\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy.

For the boundary term, (
E.35
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}dS(z)\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}|(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(-{\cal T},x+z)|

(E.37)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\,\frac{(1+{\cal T})^{2\delta}}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}\,\frac{dS(z)}{(1+{\cal T}+|x+z|)^{2}(1+(|x+z|-{\cal T})_{+})^{2\gamma}}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}.

On the domain of integration we have
|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\geq 2{\cal T}
,
and hence
|x+z|-{\cal T}\geq|x+z|/2
. Therefore taking
\gamma\geq 1/2
,
the right-hand side of (
E.37
) is bounded by

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\,\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}\,\frac{dS(z)}{(1+{\cal T}+|x+z|)^{2}(1+|x+z|/2)^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\,\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}\,\frac{dS(z)}{|x+z|^{3}}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|},

where Lemma
5.6
for
\sigma=0
entered in the last step.
For the main term in (
E.36
), we invoke again (
E.35
).
This gives

\displaystyle\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{|\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)|}{|x-y|}\,dy

(E.38)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{(1+|t-|x-y||)^{2\delta}}{(1+|t-|x-y||+|y|)^{2}(1+(|y|-|t-|x-y||)_{+})^{2\gamma}|x-y|}\,dy

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T}}\frac{dy}{|x-y|}\,\frac{(1+|\tau|)^{2\delta}}{(1+|\tau|+\lambda)^{2}(1+(\lambda-|\tau|)_{+})^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\int_{-{\cal T}}^{t}d\tau\,(1+|\tau|)^{2\delta}\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}d\lambda\,\frac{\lambda}{(1+|\tau|+\lambda)^{2}(1+(\lambda-|\tau|)_{+})^{2\gamma}},

where we wrote
\tau=t-|x-y|
and
\lambda=|y|

and used (
E.54
) in the last step.
The right-hand side is split up according to

\displaystyle\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\int_{-{\cal T}}^{0}d\tau\,(1+|\tau|)^{2\delta}\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}d\lambda\,\frac{\lambda}{(1+|\tau|+\lambda)^{2}(1+(\lambda-|\tau|)_{+})^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle+\,\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\,(1+\tau)^{2\delta}\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}d\lambda\,{\bf 1}_{\{\lambda\geq\tau\}}\,\frac{\lambda}{(1+\tau+\lambda)^{2}(1+\lambda-\tau)^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle+\,\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\,(1+\tau)^{2\delta}\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}d\lambda\,{\bf 1}_{\{\lambda\leq\tau\}}\,\frac{\lambda}{(1+\tau+\lambda)^{2}}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle A_{1}(t,x)+A_{2}(t,x)+A_{3}(t,x).

For the first term,

A_{1}(t,x)|\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\,(1+{\cal T})^{2\delta}\int_{-{\cal T}}^{0}d\tau\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}\frac{\lambda}{(1+\lambda)^{2}}\,d\lambda.

Now

\displaystyle\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}\frac{\lambda}{(1+\lambda)^{2}}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}\frac{\lambda}{(1+\lambda^{2})}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\Big(\ln(1+(|x|+t-\tau)^{2})-\ln(1+(||x|-t+\tau|)^{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\ln(1+(|x|+t-\tau)^{2})\leq C\ln|x|,

due to
\tau\in[-{\cal T},0]
. Therefore we arrive at

A_{1}(t,x)\leq C\rho^{2}\,\frac{\ln|x|}{|x|}.

For the second term, if we take
\gamma\geq 3/4
for instance, then

\displaystyle A_{2}(t,x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\,\int_{0}^{t}\,d\tau\,(1+\tau)^{2\delta}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}d\lambda\,\,\frac{\lambda}{(1+\tau+\lambda)^{2}(1+\lambda-\tau)^{3/2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\,\int_{0}^{t}\,d\tau\,\frac{(1+\tau)^{2\delta}}{1+\tau}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}d\lambda\,\,\frac{1}{(1+\lambda-\tau)^{3/2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\,\int_{0}^{t}\,d\tau\,\frac{(1+\tau)^{2\delta}}{1+\tau}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\,(1+t)^{2\delta}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|^{1-2\delta}},

the latter since
|t-|x||\leq 1
and
|x|\geq 10
. For the third term,

A_{3}(t,x)\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\,\int_{0}^{t}\,d\tau\,\frac{(1+\tau)^{2\delta}}{(1+\tau)^{2}}\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}d\lambda\,{\bf 1}_{\{\lambda\leq\tau\}}\,\lambda.

On the domain of
\lambda
-integration we have
\lambda\leq\tau
.
Moreover,
\lambda\geq||x|-t+\tau|\geq\tau-||x|-t|\geq\tau-1
.
Hence the domain of
\lambda
-integration is an interval of length at most
1
.
This yields

A_{3}(t,x)\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\,\int_{0}^{t}\,d\tau\,\frac{(1+\tau)^{2\delta}\tau}{(1+\tau)^{2}}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|^{1-2\delta}}.

Taking together all the estimates in this section so far,
we have shown that

|\partial_{t}I_{3}|\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|^{1-2\delta}}.

What concerns the
\partial_{x^{k}}
-derivatives, we first consider

\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,1}=-\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}dS(z)\,\frac{z_{k}}{|z|}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\leq{\cal S}\}}\,(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(-{\cal T},x+z).

Here (
E.35
) results in

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}dS(z)\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\leq{\cal S}\}}|(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(-{\cal T},x+z)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\,\frac{(1+{\cal T})^{2\delta}}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}\,\frac{dS(z)}{(1+{\cal T}+|x+z|)^{2}(1+(|x+z|-{\cal T})_{+})^{2\gamma}}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\leq{\cal S}\}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\,\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}\,\frac{dS(z)}{|x+z|^{2}}\,\frac{{\cal S}}{|x+z|}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|},

the latter due to Lemma
5.6
for
\sigma=0
. Next, for

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T}}\frac{\partial_{x^{k}}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy

we can apply (
E.35
) once more to bound

\displaystyle|\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,2}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T}}\frac{dy}{|x-y|}\,\frac{(1+|t-|x-y||)^{2\delta}}{(1+|t-|x-y||+|y|)^{2}(1+(|y|-|t-|x-y||)_{+})^{2\gamma}}.

However, this is the same term as in (
E.38
). Hence we obtain

|\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,2}|\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|^{1-2\delta}}.

Lastly,

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},|y|\leq{\cal S}}\bigg(\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}+\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)\bigg)\,\frac{x_{k}-y_{k}}{|x-y|^{2}}\,dy.

If
|y|\leq{\cal S}
, then
|x-y|\geq|x|-|y|\geq|x|/2
.
Furthermore,
|\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi}|+|\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})|\leq C\rho^{2}

by (
E.35
). Thus

\displaystyle|\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,3}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\int_{|y|\leq{\cal S}}\bigg(\frac{1}{|x-y|}+1\bigg)\,\frac{1}{|x-y|}\,dy\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}.

So if we summarize the bounds on
I_{3}
,
we have shown that if
\gamma\geq 3/4
and (
E.35
) holds, then

|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}I_{3}|\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|^{1-2\delta}},\quad\alpha=0,1,2,3.

(E.39)

Case III.
Here we assume that

\displaystyle\Big(|\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi}|+|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})|\Big)(t,x)

(E.40)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(1+|t|)\}}\,\frac{(1+|t|)^{\delta}}{(1+|t|+|x|)(1+(|x|-|t|)_{+})^{\gamma}}\,\frac{1}{|x|},\quad\alpha,\lambda=0,1,2,3.

From (
E.30
) we once again have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}I_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}dS(z)\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(-{\cal T},x+z)

(E.41)

\displaystyle+\,\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy.

For the boundary term, (
E.40
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}dS(z)\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}|(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(-{\cal T},x+z)|

(E.42)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\,\frac{(1+{\cal T})^{\delta}}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}\,dS(z)\,\frac{1}{|x+z|}\,\frac{1}{(1+{\cal T}+|x+z|)(1+(|x+z|-{\cal T})_{+})^{\gamma}}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}.\qquad

On the domain of integration we have
|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\geq 2{\cal T}
,
and hence
|x+z|-{\cal T}\geq|x+z|/2
.
Therefore the right-hand side of (
E.42
) is bounded by

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\,\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}\,\frac{dS(z)}{|x+z|(1+{\cal T}+|x+z|)(1+|x+z|/2)^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\,\frac{1}{t+T}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}\,\frac{dS(z)}{|x+z|^{2+\gamma}}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|^{\gamma}},

where in the last step Lemma
5.6
has been used.
For the main term in (
E.41
), once again (
E.40
) is helpful.
Since
g(|y|)\leq\frac{C}{|y|}
for
|y|\geq{\cal S}
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{|\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)|}{|x-y|}\,dy

(E.43)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{1}{|y|}\,\frac{(1+|t-|x-y||)^{\delta}}{(1+|t-|x-y||+|y|)(1+(|y|-|t-|x-y||)_{+})^{\gamma}|x-y|}\,dy

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T}}\frac{dy}{|x-y|}\,\frac{(1+|\tau|)^{\delta}}{\lambda(1+|\tau|+\lambda)(1+(\lambda-|\tau|)_{+})^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\int_{-{\cal T}}^{t}d\tau\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}d\lambda\,\frac{(1+|\tau|)^{\delta}}{(1+|\tau|+\lambda)(1+(\lambda-|\tau|)_{+})^{\gamma}},

where we wrote
\tau=t-|x-y|
and
\lambda=|y|
, and simplified by means of (
E.54
)
in the last step. The right-hand side is split up according to

\displaystyle\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\int_{-{\cal T}}^{0}d\tau\,(1+|\tau|)^{\delta}\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}d\lambda\,\frac{1}{(1+|\tau|+\lambda)(1+(\lambda-|\tau|)_{+})^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle+\,\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\,(1+\tau)^{\delta}\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}d\lambda\,{\bf 1}_{\{\lambda\geq\tau\}}\,\frac{1}{(1+\tau+\lambda)(1+\lambda-\tau)^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle+\,\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\,(1+\tau)^{\delta}\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}d\lambda\,{\bf 1}_{\{\lambda\leq\tau\}}\,\frac{1}{1+\tau+\lambda}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle B_{1}(t,x)+B_{2}(t,x)+B_{3}(t,x).

For the first term,

B_{1}(t,x)|\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\,(1+{\cal T})^{\delta}\int_{-{\cal T}}^{0}d\tau\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}\frac{d\lambda}{1+\lambda}.

Now

\displaystyle\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}\frac{d\lambda}{1+\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ln(1+(|x|+t-\tau))-\ln(1+(||x|-t+\tau|))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\ln(1+(|x|+t-\tau))\leq C\ln|x|,

due to
\tau\in[-{\cal T},0]
. Therefore we see that

B_{1}(t,x)\leq C\rho^{2}\,\frac{\ln|x|}{|x|}.

The second term is

\displaystyle B_{2}(t,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\int_{0}^{\min\{t,\frac{1}{2}(|x|+t)\}}d\tau\,(1+\tau)^{\delta}\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}d\lambda\,{\bf 1}_{\{\lambda\geq\tau\}}\,\frac{1}{(1+\tau+\lambda)(1+\lambda-\tau)^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\int_{0}^{\min\{t,\frac{1}{2}(|x|+t)\}}d\tau\,\frac{(1+\tau)^{\delta}}{1+\tau}\int_{\tau}^{|x|+t-\tau}\frac{d\lambda}{(1+\lambda-\tau)^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\int_{0}^{\min\{t,\frac{1}{2}(|x|+t)\}}d\tau\,\frac{(1+\tau)^{\delta}}{1+\tau}\,(1+|x|+t-2\tau)^{1-\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\,|x|^{1-\gamma}\int_{0}^{C|x|}d\tau\,\frac{(1+\tau)^{\delta}}{1+\tau}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|^{\gamma}}\,|x|^{\delta}.

For the third term,

\displaystyle B_{3}(t,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\,(1+\tau)^{\delta}\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}d\lambda\,{\bf 1}_{\{\lambda\leq\tau\}}\,\frac{1}{1+\tau+\lambda}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\,\int_{0}^{t}\,d\tau\,\frac{(1+\tau)^{\delta}}{1+\tau}\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}d\lambda\,{\bf 1}_{\{\lambda\leq\tau\}}.

On the domain of
\lambda
-integration we have
\lambda\leq\tau
.
Moreover,
\lambda\geq||x|-t+\tau|\geq\tau-||x|-t|\geq\tau-1
.
Hence the domain of
\lambda
-integration is an interval of length at most
1
.
This yields

B_{3}(t,x)\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\,\int_{0}^{t}\frac{d\tau}{(1+\tau)^{1-\delta}}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}\,(1+t)^{\delta}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|^{1-\delta}}.

In summary, we have shown that

|\partial_{t}I_{3}|\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|^{\gamma-\delta}}.

We still need to bound the
