\partial_{x^{k}}
-derivatives.
For, we first consider

\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,1}=-\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}dS(z)\,\frac{z_{k}}{|z|}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\leq{\cal S}\}}\,(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(-{\cal T},x+z).

Then by (
E.40
)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,1}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\,\frac{(1+{\cal T})^{\delta}}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}\,\frac{dS(z)}{|x+z|}\,\frac{1}{(1+{\cal T}+|x+z|)(1+(|x+z|-{\cal T})_{+})^{\gamma}}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\leq{\cal S}\}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\,\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}\,dS(z)\,\frac{1}{|x+z|^{2}}\,\frac{{\cal S}}{|x+z|}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|},

the latter by Lemma
5.6
for
\sigma=0
. Next, for

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T}}\frac{\partial_{x^{k}}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy

we can use (
E.40
) to bound

\displaystyle|\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,2}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{2}\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T}}\frac{dy}{|x-y|}\,\frac{(1+|t-|x-y||)^{\delta}}{|y|(1+|t-|x-y||+|y|)(1+(|y|-|t-|x-y||)_{+})^{\gamma}}

This term agrees with (
E.43
), so we obtain the estimate

|\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,2}|\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|^{\gamma-\delta}}.

Lastly,

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},|y|\leq{\cal S}}\bigg(\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\hskip 80.00012pt+\,\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)\bigg)\,\frac{x_{k}-y_{k}}{|x-y|^{2}}\,dy.

If
|y|\leq{\cal S}
, then
|x-y|\geq|x|-|y|\geq|x|/2
.
In addition, by (
E.40
) in particular

|(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t,x)|+|\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t,x)|\leq C\rho^{2}\,{\bf 1}_{\{r\geq\frac{1}{2}(1+|t|)\}}\,(1+|t|)^{\delta}\,\frac{1}{|x|}\leq C\rho^{2},

owing to
||x|-t|\leq 1
. This leads to

|\partial_{x^{k}}I_{3,3}|\leq C\rho^{2}\int_{|y|\leq{\cal S}}\bigg(\frac{1}{|x-y|}+1\bigg)\,\frac{1}{|x-y|}\,dy\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}.

So if we summarize the bounds on
I_{3}
,
we have shown that (
E.40
) implies that

|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}I_{3}|\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|^{\gamma-\delta}},\quad\alpha=0,1,2,3.

(E.44)

Hence our results for
I_{3}
can be summarized as follows.

Corollary 5.3

Let
\gamma\in[\frac{3}{4},1)
and
\delta>0

be such that
\gamma+\delta\leq 1
. If

(a)

(
E.31
)-(
E.32
) holds, or

(b)

(
E.35
) holds, or

(c)

(
E.40
) holds,

then

\bigg|\,\partial_{x^{\alpha}}\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy\bigg|\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|^{\gamma-\delta}}

for
\alpha,\lambda=0,1,2,3
.

Proof :
This follows from (
E.34
), (
E.39
) and (
E.44
).

\Box

E.2.2
I_{2}

Now we are going to bound

\partial_{x^{\alpha}}I_{2},\quad I_{2}(t,x)=\int_{|x-y|\geq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy,\quad\alpha,\lambda=0,1,2,3.

In this section we will assume that for some
l\in\{0,1\}

\displaystyle\psi(t,x)=q(x),\quad t\leq-{\cal T},

\displaystyle|\partial^{\alpha}q(x)|\leq\frac{C\rho}{|x|^{l+1+|\alpha|}},\quad|x|\geq{\cal S},\,\,|\alpha|\leq 2,

(E.45)

and for some
m\in\{0,1\}

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}(t,x)=\tilde{q}(x),\quad t\leq-{\cal T},

\displaystyle|\partial^{\alpha}\tilde{q}(x)|\leq\frac{C\rho}{|x|^{m+1+|\alpha|}},\quad|x|\geq{\cal S},\,\,|\alpha|\leq 2,

(E.46)

is verified. Since
t-|x-y|\leq-{\cal T}
on the domain of integration, this yields

I_{2}(t,x)=\int_{|x-y|\geq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{q(y)\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{q}(y)}{|x-y|}\,dy.

For the
\partial_{t}
-derivative, we write

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|z|\geq t+{\cal T},\,|x+z|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{q(x+z)\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{q}(x+z)}{|z|}\,dz

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{t+{\cal T}}^{\infty}d\sigma\int_{|z|=\sigma}dS(z)\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}\,\frac{q(x+z)\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{q}(x+z)}{\sigma}

to get

\partial_{t}I_{2}=-\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}dS(z)\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}\,q(x+z)\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{q}(x+z).

Hence, by (
E.2.2
) and (
E.2.2
),

|\partial_{t}I_{2}|\leq C\rho^{2}\,\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}\,\frac{dS(z)}{|x+z|^{3+l+m}}\leq C\rho^{2}\,\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}\,\frac{dS(z)}{|x+z|^{3}}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|},

see (
E.33
). For the
x^{k}
-derivatives,
we obtain, completely analogous to Lemma
5.10
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{x^{k}}I_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{|x-y|\geq t+{\cal T}}\frac{|\partial_{x^{k}}(q\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{q})(y)|}{|x-y|}\,dy+\int_{|x-y|\geq t+{\cal T},|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{|(q\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{q})(y)|}{|x-y|^{2}}\,dy

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}\,|(q\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{q})(x+z)|\,dS(z).

On the first domain of integration we have
|y|\geq|x-y|-t+t-|x|\geq{\cal T}-1\geq 1
.
In addition, on the second domain we may use that

1+|t-|x-y||+|y|\leq 1+|t-|x||+||x|-|x-y||+|y|\leq 2+2|y|\leq 3|y|
.
Therefore by (
E.2.2
) and (
E.2.2
)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x^{k}}I_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\rho^{2}}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|y|\geq 1}\frac{dy}{|y|^{4+l+m}}+C\rho^{2}\int_{|x-y|\geq t+{\cal T},|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{dy}{|y|^{3+l+m}|x-y|^{2}}

\displaystyle+\,\frac{C\rho^{2}}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\geq{\cal S}\}}\,\frac{dS(z)}{|x+z|^{3+l+m}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}+C\rho^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{dy}{(1+|t-|x-y||+|y|)^{3}|x-y|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}+C\rho^{2}\,\frac{1}{1+|t|+|x|}\,\frac{1}{1+|t-|x||}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|},

see (
E.33
) and (
E.55
).
Hence we have shown that (
E.2.2
) and (
E.2.2
) imply that

\bigg|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}\int_{|x-y|\geq t+{\cal T},|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy\bigg|\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}.

(E.47)

E.2.3
I_{1}

It remains to deal with

\partial_{x^{\alpha}}I_{1},\quad I_{1}(t,x)=\int_{|y|\leq{\cal S}}\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy,\quad\alpha,\lambda=0,1,2,3,

which is the easiest part. Throughout this section we will assume that

|(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t,x)|+|\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t,x)|\leq C\rho^{2}.

(E.48)

Due to (
E.48
), and since
|x-y|\geq|x|-|y|\geq|x|/2
for
|y|\leq{\cal S}
,

|\partial_{t}I_{1}(t,x)|=\bigg|\int_{|y|\leq{\cal S}}\frac{\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy\,\bigg|\leq C\rho^{2}\int_{|y|\leq{\cal S}}\frac{dy}{|x-y|}\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}.

For the
x^{k}
-derivatives, we calculate

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{k}}I_{1}(t,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{|y|\leq{\cal S}}\bigg(\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|^{3}}(x^{k}-y^{k})

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+\,\frac{\partial_{t}(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|^{2}}(x^{k}-y^{k})\bigg)\,dy

and proceed analogously, once again using (
E.48
), to find

|\partial_{x^{k}}I_{1}(t,x)|\leq C\int_{|y|\leq{\cal S}}\bigg(\frac{1}{|x-y|^{2}}+\frac{1}{|x-y|}\bigg)\,dy\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}.

Thus altogether we have seen that (
E.48
) implies that

\bigg|\partial_{x^{\alpha}}\int_{|y|\leq{\cal S}}\frac{(\psi\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\tilde{\psi})(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy\bigg|\leq\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|x|}.

(E.49)

E.3
Miscellaneous lemmas

Lemma 5.4

For
j,k,n,m\in\{1,2,3\}
we have

\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}x^{j}x^{k}\,dS(x)=\frac{4\pi r^{4}}{3}\,\delta_{jk}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}x^{j}x^{k}x^{n}x^{m}\,dS(x)=\frac{4\pi r^{6}}{15}\,(\delta_{jk}\delta_{nm}+\delta_{jn}\delta_{km}+\delta_{jm}\delta_{kn}).

Proof :
Consider the first integral. If
j\neq k
, the result is zero because then the change
of variable
x^{j}\mapsto-x^{j}
would cause the integrand to be multiplied by
-1

while maintaining the domain of integration. So we only need to study the case
j=k
,
and there are
3
different integrals under this case:

\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}(x^{1})^{2}\,dS(x)=\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}(x^{2})^{2}\,dS(x)=\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}(x^{3})^{2}\,dS(x)\ .

They are all equal by symmetry. Therefore

\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}\big((x^{1})^{2}+(x^{2})^{2}+(x^{3})^{2}\big)\,dS(x)=r^{2}\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}\,dS(x)=4\pi r^{4}\ .

Hence each of the
3
integrals has value
4\pi r^{4}/3
.

Now consider the second integral. Similarly to the above, if there exists an index from
\{1,2,3\}

that only appears once among
\{j,k,n,m\}
, then
\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}x^{j}x^{k}x^{n}x^{m}\,dS(x)=0
.
This means that the only such integrals that are nonzero are the following ones (and the equality among
them is again due to symmetry):

I_{1}=\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}(x^{1})^{4}\,dS(x)=\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}(x^{2})^{4}\,dS(x)=\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}(x^{3})^{4}\,dS(x)

and

I_{2}=\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}(x^{1}x^{2})^{2}\,dS(x)=\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}(x^{1}x^{3})^{2}\,dS(x)=\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}(x^{2}x^{3})^{2}\,dS(x)\ .

Note that

\displaystyle 3I_{1}+6I_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}\big((x^{1})^{4}+(x^{2})^{4}+(x^{3})^{4}+2(x^{1}x^{2})^{2}+2(x^{1}x^{3})^{2}+2(x^{2}x^{3})^{2}\big)\,dS(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}\big((x^{1})^{2}+(x^{2})^{2}+(x^{3})^{2}\big)^{2}\,dS(x)=r^{4}\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}\,dS(x)=4\pi r^{6}.

(E.50)

Now we will find the ratio between
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
. In spherical coordinates,
we can transform
I_{1}
into a multiple of the integral
\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\sin^{4}\theta\cos^{4}\phi\,d\phi\,d\theta
and
I_{2}
into that same multiple
of the integral
\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\sin^{4}\theta\cos^{2}\phi\sin^{2}\phi\,d\phi\,d\theta
,
hence all we need is to find out the ratio between the following integrals:

J_{1}=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\cos^{4}\phi\,d\phi\ ,\quad J_{2}=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\cos^{2}\phi\sin^{2}\phi\,d\phi\ ;

they are evaluated as
J_{1}=\frac{3\pi}{4}
and
J_{2}=\frac{\pi}{4}
.
Therefore
J_{1}=3J_{2}
and thus also
I_{1}=3I_{2}
. Together with (
E.3
), we can conclude

I_{1}=\frac{4\pi r^{6}}{5}\ ,\quad I_{2}=\frac{4\pi r^{6}}{15}\ ,

which can be written more compactly using the Dirac delta as in the statement of the lemma.

\Box

Lemma 5.5

(a) If
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, then

|\det(I+tA)-1-t\,{\rm tr}A|\leq(2^{n}+n!)(1+|A|)^{n}\,t^{2}

(E.51)

for
|t|\leq 1
. In particular, if
A=A(x)
is a matrix-valued function
such that
|A(x)|\leq\varphi(x)
for some function
0<\varphi(x)\leq 1
, then

|\det(I+A(x))-1-{\rm tr}A(x)|\leq 2^{n}(2^{n}+n!)\,\varphi(x)^{2}.

(E.52)

(b) We have

\sqrt{1+x}=1+\frac{1}{2}\,x-\frac{1}{8}\,x^{2}+{\cal O}_{1}(x^{3})

for
|x|\leq 1/2
, where
|{\cal O}_{1}(x^{3})|\leq|x|^{3}
.

Proof :
(a) For (
E.51
) let
C=I+tA
, so that
c_{ij}=\delta_{ij}+ta_{ij}
. Then

\det(I+tA)=(1+ta_{11})\cdot\ldots\cdot(1+ta_{nn})+\sum_{\pi\in{\cal S}_{n},\,\pi\neq{\rm id}}\sigma(\pi)c_{1\pi(1)}c_{2\pi(2)}\ldots c_{n\pi(n)}.

Notice that
(1+ta_{11})\cdot\ldots\cdot(1+ta_{nn})
can be evaluated to yield
2^{n}
terms.
Among them is
1
, and there are
ta_{11}+\ldots+ta_{nn}=t\,{\rm tr}A
. All the other terms
have a factor
t^{k}
for some
k\geq 2
and a product of
k

a_{ii}
’s [at least two,
but at most
n
of them]. Since
|t^{k}|\leq t^{2}
due to
|t|\leq 1

and
|a_{i_{1}i_{1}}\ldots a_{i_{k}i_{k}}|\leq\|A\|^{k}\leq(1+\|A\|)^{n}
,
it follows that

\Big|(1+ta_{11})\cdot\ldots\cdot(1+ta_{nn})-1-t\,{\rm tr}A\Big|\leq 2^{n}(1+\|A\|)^{n}\,t^{2}.

Furthermore, if
\pi\in{\cal S}_{n}
is such that
\pi\neq{\rm id}
, then
\pi(i)\neq i

for at least two
i
, say
i_{1}\neq i_{2}
. But then

|c_{1\pi(1)}c_{2\pi(2)}\ldots c_{n\pi(n)}|\leq(1+|t|\|A\|)^{n-2}\,t^{2}\,|a_{i_{1}\pi(i_{1})}|\,|a_{i_{2}\pi(i_{2})}|\leq(1+\|A\|)^{n}\,t^{2},

which yields (
E.51
). From this we get (
E.52
)
by replacing
t
with
\varphi(x)
and
A
with
A(x)/\varphi(x)
.
(b) The Taylor expansion of
\phi(x)=\sqrt{1+x}
is

\phi(x)=\phi(0)+\phi^{\prime}(0)\,x+\frac{1}{2}\,\phi^{\prime\prime}(0)\,x^{2}+\frac{1}{6}\,x^{3}\,\phi^{\prime\prime\prime}(\xi)

for some
|\xi|\leq 1/2
. Then

|{\cal O}_{1}(x^{3})|\leq\frac{1}{6}\,|x|^{3}\,\cdot\frac{3}{8}\,\max_{|\xi|\leq 1/2}\frac{1}{(1+\xi)^{5/2}}=2^{-3/2}\,|x|^{3}\leq|x|^{3},

as claimed.

\Box

Lemma 5.6

For
\sigma\in[0,1[
we have

\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}\frac{dS(z)}{|x+z|^{3-\sigma}}\leq\frac{C}{|x|^{1-\sigma}}.

(E.53)

Proof :
First we take
\sigma=0
. Then we can use a rotation of
x
to
re_{3}
to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+T}\frac{dS(z)}{|x+z|^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+T}\frac{dS(z)}{|re_{3}+z|^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(t+{\cal T})\int_{|\omega|=1}\frac{dS(\omega)}{|re_{3}+(t+{\cal T})\omega|^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\,(t+{\cal T})\int_{0}^{\pi}d\theta\,\sin\theta\,\frac{1}{((t+{\cal T})^{2}+2(t+{\cal T})\cos\theta\,r+r^{2})^{3/2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\,(t+{\cal T})\int_{-1}^{1}ds\,\frac{1}{((t+{\cal T})^{2}+2(t+{\cal T})s\,r+r^{2})^{3/2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C}{r}\Big(\frac{1}{((t+{\cal T})^{2}-2(t+{\cal T})\,r+r^{2})^{1/2}}-\frac{1}{((t+{\cal T})^{2}+2(t+{\cal T})s\,r+r^{2})^{1/2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C}{r}\Big(\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}-r}-\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}+r}\Big)=C\,\frac{1}{(t+{\cal T}-r)(t+{\cal T}+r)}.

Since
t+{\cal T}-r\geq 2+t-|x|\geq 1
and
t+{\cal T}+r\geq r=|x|
,
we obtain (
E.53
) for
\sigma=0
. If
|z|=t+{\cal T}
,
then
|z|\leq 1+|x|+{\cal T}\leq 3|x|
yields

\frac{1}{|x+z|^{3-\sigma}}=\frac{|x+z|^{\sigma}}{|x+z|^{3}}\leq C\,\frac{|x|^{\sigma}}{|x+z|^{3}},

so that the general case follows from the case
\sigma=0
.

\Box

Lemma 5.7

We have

\int_{a\leq|x-y|\leq b}\frac{dy}{|x-y|}\,g(t-|x-y|,|y|)=\frac{2\pi}{|x|}\,\int_{t-b}^{t-a}\,d\tau\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}d\lambda\,\lambda\,g(\tau,\lambda).

(E.54)

Proof :
See
[
7
, Lemma 7]
or
[
4
, Lemma A.1]
.

\Box

Lemma 5.8

We have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{dy}{|x-y|^{2}}\,\frac{1}{(1+|t-|x-y||+|y|)^{3}}\leq C\,\frac{1}{1+|t|+|x|}\,\frac{1}{1+|t-|x||}.

(E.55)

Proof :
See
[
4
, Lemma 3]
for
n=2
and
q=3
.

\Box

Lemma 5.9

Let
h:\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a sufficiently smooth and decaying function.
For
a,b>0
define

H(x)=\int_{|y|\leq a,|y-x|\leq b}h(x,y)\,dy.

Then the (distributional) derivative of
H
is

\nabla H(x)=\int_{|y|\leq a,|x-y|\leq b}\nabla_{1}h(x,y)\,dy-\int_{|z|=b}\frac{z}{|z|}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x-z|\leq a\}}\,h(x,x-z)\,dS(z),

where
\nabla_{1}
denotes the gradient with respect to the first variable.

Proof :
For
\varphi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})

we calculate by means of the divergence theorem

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}H(x)\nabla\varphi(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dx\,\int_{|y|\leq a}dy\,{\bf 1}_{\{|y-x|\leq b\}}\,h(x,y)\,\nabla\varphi(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|y|\leq a}dy\int_{|z|\leq b}dz\,h(y+z,y)\,\nabla_{z}[\varphi(y+z)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|y|\leq a}dy\,\bigg(\int_{|z|=b}dS(z)\,h(y+z,y)\varphi(y+z)\,\frac{z}{|z|}-\int_{|z|\leq b}dz\,\nabla_{1}h(y+z,y)\,\varphi(y+z)\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|z|=b}dS(z)\,\frac{z}{|z|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dx\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x-z|\leq a\}}\,h(x,x-z)\varphi(x)-\int_{|z|\leq b}dz\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dx\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x-z|\leq a\}}\nabla_{1}h(x,x-z)\,\varphi(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dx\bigg(\int_{|z|\leq b}dz\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x-z|\leq a\}}\nabla_{1}h(x,x-z)-\int_{|z|=b}dS(z)\,\frac{z}{|z|}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x-z|\leq a\}}\,h(x,x-z)\bigg)\varphi(x)\,dx,

and the assertion follows.

\Box

Lemma 5.10

For sufficiently smooth and decaying
g
, and
k=1,2,3
,

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{k}}\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\geq{\cal S}}\frac{g(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T}}\frac{\partial_{x^{k}}g(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy

\displaystyle+\,\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},|y|\leq{\cal S}}\bigg(\frac{g(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}+\partial_{t}g(t-|x-y|,y)\bigg)\,\frac{x_{k}-y_{k}}{|x-y|^{2}}\,dy

\displaystyle-\,\frac{1}{t+{\cal T}}\int_{|z|=t+{\cal T}}\frac{z_{k}}{|z|}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x+z|\leq{\cal S}\}}\,g(-{\cal T},x+z)\,dS(z).

Proof :
Let
I
denote the integral in question. Then

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T}}\frac{g(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy-\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\leq{\cal S}}\frac{g(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|z|\leq t+{\cal T}}\frac{g(t-|z|,x-z)}{|z|}\,dz-\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\leq{\cal S}}\frac{g(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy.

Therefore

\partial_{x^{k}}I=\int_{|z|\leq t+{\cal T}}\frac{\partial_{x^{k}}g(t-|z|,x-z)}{|z|}\,dz-\partial_{x^{k}}\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T},\,|y|\leq{\cal S}}\frac{g(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}\,dy.

For the second term we are going to use Lemma
5.9

with
a={\cal S}
,
b=t+{\cal T}
and
h(x,y)=\frac{g(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}
. Then

\nabla_{1}h(x,y)=-\bigg(\frac{g(t-|x-y|,y)}{|x-y|}+\partial_{t}g(t-|x-y|,y)\bigg)\,\frac{x-y}{|x-y|^{2}},

which yields the claim.

\Box

Lemma 5.11

We have

\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T}}\frac{dy}{|x-y||y|^{4}}\,{\bf 1}_{\{|y|\geq\frac{1}{2}(1+|t-|x-y||)\}}\leq C(1+{\cal T})\,\frac{1}{|x|}.

Proof :
Since
|y|\geq 1/2
on the domain of integration,
also
|y|\geq\frac{1}{3}(1+|y|)
. Hence, by (
E.54
),
the integral is bounded by

\displaystyle C\int_{|x-y|\leq t+{\cal T}}\frac{dy}{|x-y|(1+|y|)^{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|x|}\int_{-{\cal T}}^{t}\,d\tau\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}d\lambda\,\frac{\lambda}{(1+\lambda)^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|x|}\int_{-{\cal T}}^{t}\,d\tau\int_{||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}\frac{d\lambda}{(1+\lambda)^{3}}.

If
|x|-t+\tau\leq 0
, then
\tau\leq t-|x|\leq 1
, so that

\frac{C}{|x|}\int_{-{\cal T}}^{t}\,d\tau\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x|-t+\tau\leq 0\}}\int_{t-|x|-\tau}^{|x|+t-\tau}\frac{d\lambda}{(1+\lambda)^{3}}\leq\frac{C}{|x|}\int_{-{\cal T}}^{1}\,d\tau\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\lambda}{(1+\lambda)^{3}}\leq\frac{C(1+{\cal T})}{|x|}.

On the other hand, if
|x|-t+\tau\geq 0
, then

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|x|}\int_{-{\cal T}}^{t}\,d\tau\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x|-t+\tau\geq 0\}}\int_{|x|-t+\tau}^{|x|+t-\tau}\frac{d\lambda}{(1+\lambda)^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|x|}\int_{-{\cal T}}^{t}\,d\tau\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x|-t+\tau\geq 0\}}\bigg(\frac{1}{(1+|x|-t+\tau)^{2}}-\frac{1}{(1+|x|+t-\tau)^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|x|}\int_{-{\cal T}}^{t}\,d\tau\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x|-t+\tau\geq 0\}}\frac{(1+|x|+t-\tau)^{2}-(1+|x|-t+\tau)^{2}}{(1+|x|-t+\tau)^{2}(1+|x|+t-\tau)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|x|}\int_{-{\cal T}}^{t}\,d\tau\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x|-t+\tau\geq 0\}}\frac{(1+|x|)(t-\tau)}{(1+|x|-t+\tau)^{2}(1+|x|+t-\tau)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{-{\cal T}}^{t}\,d\tau\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x|-t+\tau\geq 0\}}\frac{t-\tau}{(1+|x|-t+\tau)^{2}(1+|x|+t-\tau)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{t+{\cal T}}\,ds\,{\bf 1}_{\{|x|\geq s\}}\frac{s}{(1+|x|-s)^{2}(1+|x|+s)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{1+|x|}\int_{0}^{|x|}\,\frac{ds}{(1+|x|-s)^{2}}=\frac{C}{1+|x|}\int_{0}^{|x|}\,\frac{d\sigma}{(1+\sigma)^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{1+|x|}.

Taking together both estimates, we obtain the claim.

\Box

The following multipole expansion corresponds
to
[
14
, (27.19)-(27.21)]
, with the error terms included.

Lemma 5.12

Let
x,\bar{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
be such that
\bar{r}\leq r_{\ast}

and
r\geq 5r_{\ast}
for some
r_{\ast}\geq 1
, where
r=|x|
and
\bar{r}=|\bar{x}|
.

(a) We have the expansion

\displaystyle|x-\bar{x}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r-\frac{1}{r}\,(x\cdot\bar{x})+\frac{1}{2r}\bigg(\bar{r}^{2}-\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}\bigg)+{\cal O}(r^{-2}),

(E.56)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|x-\bar{x}|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{r^{3}}(x\cdot\bar{x})+\frac{1}{2r^{3}}\,\bigg(\frac{3(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}-\bar{r}^{2}\bigg)+{\cal O}(r^{-4}),

(E.57)

with the implied constants depending only on powers of
r_{\ast}
.

(b) Let
\psi=\psi(t)
be a suitable function. Then

\displaystyle\frac{\psi(t-c^{-1}|x-\bar{x}|)}{|x-\bar{x}|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{r^{3}}(x\cdot\bar{x})+\frac{1}{2r^{3}}\,\bigg(\frac{3(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}-\bar{r}^{2}\bigg)\bigg]\,\psi(t-c^{-1}r)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{c}\,\bigg[\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,(x\cdot\bar{x})+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\bigg(\frac{3(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}-\bar{r}^{2}\bigg)\bigg]\,\psi^{\prime}(t-c^{-1}r)+\frac{1}{2c^{2}}\,\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{3}}\,\psi^{\prime\prime}(t-c^{-1}r)

\displaystyle+\,{\cal O}\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{3}c^{-j}r^{-(4-j)}{\|\psi^{(j)}\|}_{\infty}\Big),

with the implied constants depending only on powers of
r_{\ast}
. In particular,

\displaystyle\frac{\psi(t-c^{-1}|x-\bar{x}|)}{|x-\bar{x}|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}\,\psi(t-c^{-1}r)+\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})}{cr^{2}}\,\psi^{\prime}(t-c^{-1}r)+\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{2c^{2}r^{3}}\,\psi^{\prime\prime}(t-c^{-1}r)

(E.58)

\displaystyle+\,{\cal O}\Big(r^{-2}\sum_{j=0}^{2}c^{-j}{\|\psi^{(j)}\|}_{\infty}+c^{-3}r^{-1}{\|\psi^{\prime\prime\prime}\|}_{\infty}\Big).

(c) Let
\psi=\psi(t)
be a suitable function. Then

\displaystyle\frac{\psi(t-c^{-1}|x-\bar{x}|)}{|x-\bar{x}|^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,\psi(t-c^{-1}r)+\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})}{cr^{3}}\,\psi^{\prime}(t-c^{-1}r)+\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{2c^{2}r^{4}}\,\psi^{\prime\prime}(t-c^{-1}r)

\displaystyle+\,{\cal O}\Big(r^{-3}\sum_{j=0}^{2}c^{-j}{\|\psi^{(j)}\|}_{\infty}+c^{-3}r^{-2}{\|\psi^{\prime\prime\prime}\|}_{\infty}\Big),

with the implied constants depending only on powers of
r_{\ast}
.

Proof :
(a) For
|\varepsilon|\leq 1/2
the function
\phi(\varepsilon)=\sqrt{1-\varepsilon}
has the expansion

\phi(\varepsilon)=\phi(0)+\phi^{\prime}(0)\,\varepsilon+\frac{1}{2}\,\phi^{\prime\prime}(0)\,\varepsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{6}\,\varepsilon^{3}\,\phi^{\prime\prime\prime}(\xi)

for some
|\xi|\leq 1/2
. It follows that

\sqrt{1-\varepsilon}=1-\frac{1}{2}\,\varepsilon-\frac{1}{8}\,\varepsilon^{2}+{\cal O}_{1}(\varepsilon^{3}),

(E.59)

where

|{\cal O}_{1}(\varepsilon^{3})|=\frac{1}{6}\,\varepsilon^{3}\,\cdot\frac{3}{8}\,\max_{|\xi|\leq 1/2}\frac{1}{(1-\xi)^{5/2}}=2^{-3/2}\,\varepsilon^{3}\leq\varepsilon^{3}.

Below we will take
\varepsilon=\frac{2}{r^{2}}\,x\cdot\bar{x}-\frac{\bar{r}^{2}}{r^{2}}
; note that

|\varepsilon|=|\frac{2}{r^{2}}\,x\cdot\bar{x}-\frac{\bar{r}^{2}}{r^{2}}|\leq\frac{2\bar{r}}{r}+\frac{\bar{r}^{2}}{r^{2}}\leq\frac{2r_{\ast}}{5r_{\ast}}+\frac{r_{\ast}^{2}}{25r_{\ast}^{2}}\leq 1/2
as well as

|\varepsilon|\leq\frac{2\bar{r}}{r}+\frac{\bar{r}^{2}}{r^{2}}\leq\frac{3r_{\ast}}{r}
.
Concerning (
E.56
), the expansion (
E.59
) yields

\displaystyle|x-\bar{x}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{r^{2}-2x\cdot\bar{x}+\bar{r}^{2}}=r\,\sqrt{1-\frac{2}{r^{2}}\,x\cdot\bar{x}+\frac{\bar{r}^{2}}{r^{2}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r-\frac{1}{2r}\,(2\,x\cdot\bar{x}-\bar{r}^{2})-\frac{1}{8r^{3}}\,(2\,x\cdot\bar{x}-\bar{r}^{2})^{2}+r\,{\cal O}_{1}\bigg(\frac{27r_{\ast}^{3}}{r^{3}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r-\frac{1}{r}\,(x\cdot\bar{x})+\frac{1}{2r}\bigg(\bar{r}^{2}-\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}\bigg)+\frac{1}{2r^{3}}\,(x\cdot\bar{x})\,\bar{r}^{2}-\frac{\bar{r}^{4}}{8r^{3}}+r\,{\cal O}_{1}\bigg(\frac{27r_{\ast}^{3}}{r^{3}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r-\frac{1}{r}\,(x\cdot\bar{x})+\frac{1}{2r}\bigg(\bar{r}^{2}-\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}\bigg)+{\cal O}_{2}(r^{-2})

with

|{\cal O}_{2}(r^{-2})|=\bigg(\frac{r_{\ast}^{3}}{2}+\frac{r_{\ast}^{4}}{40}+27r_{\ast}^{3}\bigg)r^{-2}.

Similarly, the constants in the order terms below will depend on powers
r_{\ast}
.
To verify (
E.57
), we write (
E.56
) as
|x-\bar{x}|=r+A_{0}+A_{1}+A_{2}
according
to the orders in
r^{-1}
and expand
\frac{1}{M+R}=\frac{1}{M}-\frac{R}{(M+R)M}

for a main term
M
and a remainder
R
. Then
(A_{0}+A_{1}+A_{2})^{2}=A_{0}^{2}+{\cal O}(r^{-1})
, so that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|x-\bar{x}|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}-\frac{A_{0}+A_{1}+A_{2}}{r|x-\bar{x}|}=\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{r}\,(A_{0}+A_{1}+A_{2})\,\bigg(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{A_{0}+A_{1}+A_{2}}{r|x-\bar{x}|}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,(A_{0}+A_{1}+A_{2})+\frac{(A_{0}+A_{1}+A_{2})^{2}}{r^{2}|x-\bar{x}|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,(A_{0}+A_{1})+{\cal O}(r^{-4})+\frac{A_{0}^{2}+{\cal O}(r^{-1})}{r^{2}|x-\bar{x}|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r}-\frac{A_{0}}{r^{2}}-\frac{A_{1}}{r^{2}}+\frac{A_{0}^{2}}{r^{3}}+{\cal O}(r^{-4}),

which gives (
E.57
), due
A_{0}=-\frac{1}{r}(x\cdot\bar{x})
,

A_{1}=\frac{1}{2r}(\bar{r}^{2}-\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}})
and
A_{2}={\cal O}(r^{-2})
.
(b) Let
T=t-c^{-1}|x-\bar{x}|
and
u=t-c^{-1}r
. Then, owing to (
E.56
),

T-u=\frac{1}{c}\,(|x|-|x-\bar{x}|)=\frac{1}{c}\,\bigg(\frac{1}{r}\,(x\cdot\bar{x})-\frac{1}{2r}\bigg(\bar{r}^{2}-\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}\bigg)+{\cal O}(r^{-2})\bigg)

(E.60)

and in particular
T-u=c^{-1}\,{\cal O}(1)={\cal O}(c^{-1})
. By Taylor expansion,

\displaystyle\psi(T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi(u)+\psi^{\prime}(u)\,(T-u)+\frac{1}{2}\,\psi^{\prime\prime}(u)\,(T-u)^{2}+\frac{1}{6}\,\psi^{\prime\prime\prime}(\xi)\,(T-u)^{3}

(E.61)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi(u)+\psi^{\prime}(u)\,(T-u)+\frac{1}{2}\,\psi^{\prime\prime}(u)\,(T-u)^{2}+{\cal O}({\|\psi^{\prime\prime\prime}\|}_{\infty}c^{-3}).

Using (
E.57
), (
E.61
) and (
E.60
),

\displaystyle\frac{\psi(T)}{|x-\bar{x}|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{r^{3}}(x\cdot\bar{x})+\frac{1}{2r^{3}}\,\bigg(\frac{3(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}-\bar{r}^{2}\bigg)+{\cal O}(r^{-4})\bigg]

\displaystyle\,\times\bigg(\psi(u)+\psi^{\prime}(u)\,(T-u)+\frac{1}{2}\,\psi^{\prime\prime}(u)\,(T-u)^{2}+{\cal O}({\|\psi^{\prime\prime\prime}\|}_{\infty}c^{-3})\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{r^{3}}(x\cdot\bar{x})+\frac{1}{2r^{3}}\,\bigg(\frac{3(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}-\bar{r}^{2}\bigg)\bigg]\,\psi(u)+{\cal O}({\|\psi\|}_{\infty}r^{-4})

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{c}\,\bigg[\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{r^{3}}(x\cdot\bar{x})+\frac{1}{2r^{3}}\,\bigg(\frac{3(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}-\bar{r}^{2}\bigg)+{\cal O}(r^{-4})\bigg]\,\psi^{\prime}(u)

\displaystyle\quad\times\bigg(\frac{1}{r}\,(x\cdot\bar{x})-\frac{1}{2r}\bigg(\bar{r}^{2}-\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}\bigg)+{\cal O}(r^{-2})\bigg)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{2c^{2}}\,\bigg[\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{r^{3}}(x\cdot\bar{x})+\frac{1}{2r^{3}}\,\bigg(\frac{3(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}-\bar{r}^{2}\bigg)+{\cal O}(r^{-4})\bigg]\,\psi^{\prime\prime}(u)\,\bigg(\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}+{\cal O}(r^{-1})\bigg)

\displaystyle+\,{\cal O}({\|\psi^{\prime\prime\prime}\|}_{\infty}c^{-3}r^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{r^{3}}(x\cdot\bar{x})+\frac{1}{2r^{3}}\,\bigg(\frac{3(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}-\bar{r}^{2}\bigg)\bigg]\,\psi(u)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{c}\,\bigg(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,(x\cdot\bar{x})-\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\bigg(\bar{r}^{2}-\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}\bigg)+{\cal O}(r^{-3})\bigg)\psi^{\prime}(u)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{c}\,\bigg[\frac{1}{r^{3}}(x\cdot\bar{x})+{\cal O}(r^{-3})\bigg]\,\psi^{\prime}(u)\,\bigg(\frac{1}{r}\,(x\cdot\bar{x})+{\cal O}(r^{-1})\bigg)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{2c^{2}}\,\bigg[\frac{1}{r}+{\cal O}(r^{-2})\bigg]\,\psi^{\prime\prime}(u)\,\bigg(\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}+{\cal O}(r^{-1})\bigg)

\displaystyle+\,{\cal O}({\|\psi\|}_{\infty}r^{-4})+{\cal O}({\|\psi^{\prime\prime\prime}\|}_{\infty}c^{-3}r^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{r^{3}}(x\cdot\bar{x})+\frac{1}{2r^{3}}\,\bigg(\frac{3(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}-\bar{r}^{2}\bigg)\bigg]\,\psi(u)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{c}\,\bigg(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,(x\cdot\bar{x})-\frac{1}{2r^{2}}\bigg(\bar{r}^{2}-\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{2}}\bigg)\bigg)\psi^{\prime}(u)+\frac{1}{c}\,\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{4}}\,\psi^{\prime}(u)+\frac{1}{2c^{2}}\,\psi^{\prime\prime}(u)\,\frac{(x\cdot\bar{x})^{2}}{r^{3}}

\displaystyle+\,{\cal O}({\|\psi\|}_{\infty}r^{-4})+{\cal O}({\|\psi^{\prime\prime\prime}\|}_{\infty}c^{-3}r^{-1})+{\cal O}({\|\psi^{\prime}\|}_{\infty}c^{-1}r^{-3})+{\cal O}({\|\psi^{\prime\prime}\|}_{\infty}c^{-2}r^{-2}),

and this yields the claim. (c) This follows from (
E.57
) and (b).

\Box
